# Patterns 7a-3 part A: Layouts, Trees of Placements and Previews Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** The five layout nodes of patterns spec §3 (Linear, Grid, Circular, Curve and Face Pattern), Place and the four shortcuts taking trees of placements (one `Kernel.place` and one `Kernel.boolean` for the whole tree), instance tags that carry the instance's tree path so picks follow `{0;3}` as they followed `{3}` (and warn, naming the path, when it is gone), previews of a selected layout node in the viewport, a face-menu entry that puts a Face Pattern on a face, and the headless acceptance test of spec §9.

**Architecture:** A placement's tag item stays one `Int`: `{i}` is the item `i` exactly as in 7a-2 (so saved picks resolve unchanged) and a deeper path is packed into one negative number (`InstancePath`), so `Kernel.place` is not touched; the pattern node renames the copies it gets back (`Topology.withInstanceItem`), which costs no kernel call. The pattern nodes read placements, tools and sizes whole (`.tree` access) and match them with one rule (`InstanceGrid`), so a node still runs once and makes one kernel call whatever the tree. The layouts are plain nodes returning planes (trees of depth 2 for all but Linear). Previews are an overlay (the sketch editor's mechanism), which by construction is no part of the scene: it never picks, frames or opens a menu.

**Tech Stack:** Swift 6.4 (strict concurrency), Swift Testing, OpenCascade 7.9 behind `Kernel` (`OCCTKernel.serialized`), MetalUI only through the existing viewport and `AppModel`.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-patterns-trees-design.md` (approved by the user 2026-10-10): §3 (layouts), §6 (naming), §8 (previews) and §9 (acceptance), with its Errata (7a-1 and 7a-2), the merged plans `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-10-7a-trees.md` and `2026-10-10-7a-place.md`, and the parent spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-metalcreator-vertical-slice-design.md` with all its Errata. Executors read them. Culling, Random Reduce / Values and Jitter are track 7a-variation, not here.

**Branch:** `7a-layouts` in `/Users/maxburger/Developer/MetalCreator-7a-layouts`, off master `9e5d2d5` (2,639 tests). ## Global Constraints

- `swift-tools-version: 6.4`, `swiftLanguageModes: [.v6]`: strict concurrency, data-race errors are errors. No `@unchecked Sendable`, no `nonisolated(unsafe)`.
- Tests use Swift Testing (`import Testing`, `@Test`, `#expect`), never XCTest. A full `swift test` passes only if the exit code is 0, no line says "recorded an issue" or "failed after" (Swift Testing uses glyphs, not ✘), and 11 "Test run with" lines appear.
- `swiftlint lint --strict` reports zero violations (`.swiftlint.yml`: 140 columns, trailing commas in multi-line literals, at most 6 parameters, nesting 1 level). Fix the code rather than disabling a rule; give any disable a reason.
- One type per file; `@MainActor @Observable` models hold behaviour and views are thin (this track adds no model or view); no force unwraps and no force `try`; no `DispatchQueue`; numbers in messages go through `Int.display` / `Double.display` / `FormatStyle`, never `String(format:)`; `foregroundStyle`, no `onTapGesture`, no `GeometryReader`, per `AGENTS.md` (no SwiftUI view is written here).
- OCCT stays behind `Kernel`: all OCCT work is in `Sources/CreatorOCCT` under `OCCTKernel.serialized`. `CreatorNodes`, `CreatorGraph`, `CreatorViewport` and `CreatorApp` never import it (tests may).
- MetalUI gaps go in `docs/metalui-gaps.md` (the entry and the summary-table row) and are never worked around. This track found none (User decision 13's depth-aware previews would be a change to our own viewport renderer, not to MetalUI); if you meet one, stop and log it.
- Socket names are file data (`.mcgraph` `inputValues` keys) and the inspector's visible labels: the new nodes' are spelled out (`countStep`, `startAngle`, `spacingX`), never abbreviated.
- No file-format change: `GraphFile.currentFormatVersion` stays 6 (7a-1 made it). Picks already store tags; a placed copy's are a longer form of the same data. Files of format 1 to 5 open unchanged.
- The messages are fixed by the spec: "Patterns are limited to 2,000 instances." and "Face Pattern works on flat faces for now.", and an instance is named by its path (`{4}`, `{0;3}`).
- The existing bracket and every 7a-1 and 7a-2 file must keep working unchanged. A flat list is a one-level tree: its instance `i` is `{i}` and the tag item `i`.
- Never edit `Package.swift`'s MetalUI path, never modify `../MetalUI`, never take screenshots. Create nodes only with `NodeRegistry.makeNode`; read sockets through `NodeRegistry.inputs(for:)`.
- Do not commit the plan's scratch files; the commit steps list the files.

## Review Focus

The inputs the spec implies but no happy-path test exercises, most likely to bite first. Each has a test in the task named.

1. **A tree whose branches differ in length, or have a gap** (a Face Pattern whose edge rows are empty, rows of 4 and 2 from a Partition) into Place and the shortcuts: every copy is placed, the gaps stay as empty branches, the output has the input's shape: Task 2 (`theOutputKeepsTheShapeIncludingEmptyBranches`, `aTreeWithEmptyBranchesOnlyMakesInstancesFromTheItemsItHas`), Task 3 (`rowsOfDifferentLengthsAreAllPlacedAndKeepTheirLengths`), Task 9 (`aPlaceOverTheFacePatternCopiesToolsInTheTreeOfRows`).
2. **A tree where only one value belongs** (the part, the operation) or **sizes nested differently from the placements**: a plain refusal naming what to do or both shapes, nothing placed, the kernel never called: Task 3 (`treesOfDifferentDepthsAreRefusedNamingBothShapesAndNothingIsPlaced`), Task 4 (`aTreeOnThePartIsRefusedAndNothingIsPlaced`, `aSizeNestedDifferentlyFromThePlacementsNamesBothShapes`).
3. **A file saved before trees**: a pick stored in 7a-2's flat-index form resolves with no warning, because `{i}` is the item `i` (and a flat Linear Pattern is named `{i}` too); the proof is a file the released program wrote, kept as text (`LegacyDocuments`), not one built by today's code: Task 5 (`aPickInAFileWrittenBy7a2StillResolvesAfterTheFileIsReopened`) and Task 12 (`aBracketWrittenAsFormat5OpensWithItsPicksAndVolumeUnchanged`: the bracket as format 5 wrote it), Task 13 (`aLinearPatternNamesItsHolesByIndexAndACircularOneByRingAndIndex`).
4. **Degenerate layout inputs**: a zero or negative count, a zero direction, axis or facing, a count of `Int.max` (no overflow, no hang), more than 2,000 placements (also as rings × count and as a Face Pattern with a tiny spacing), a negative ring radius or count: Tasks 6, 7, 8 and 9, the `aBadInputIsRefusedInPlainWords` tests; an edge that is neither straight nor circular: Task 13 (`anEdgeThatIsNeitherStraightNorRoundIsRefusedNamingIt`).
5. **A pick on a path that is gone** (a lower ring count, a shorter row, a Face Pattern on the floor of a hole that is gone): the node warns or refuses naming the path (several in path order), selects nothing and never takes a neighbour: Task 5 (`aPickOnAPathThatIsGoneWarnsNamingItAndSelectsNothing`, `everyVanishedPathIsNamedInPathOrder`), Task 12 (`aPickOnARingThatIsGoneWarnsNamingItsPath`), Task 13 (`aFacePatternOnTheFloorOfAHoleThatIsGoneNamesTheHole`).
6. **A vent's name when the face changes under it** (a bigger margin, a bigger hole, a narrow face with a stagger): a Face Pattern names a grid cell, not its place among the survivors, so every vent that stays keeps its path and its position, and a pick on it stays on it: Task 9 (`aVentKeepsItsPathWhenTheMarginGrowsByAPitch`, `aVentKeepsItsPathWhenAHoleGrows`, `aFaceNarrowerThanHalfAPitchStaggersWithoutImpossibleColumns`), Task 12 (`aVentKeepsItsPathAndPlaceWhenTheMarginGrows`: the faces of the slot cut at the vent are still at it).
7. **A list wired into a layout's single-valued input** (a Series into `spacingX`): refused in the layout's own words before it runs, never one run per value and never the evaluator's tree sentence: Task 13 (`aListWiredIntoALayoutIsRefusedInTheLayoutsOwnWords`).

## User decisions

Product behaviour the spec leaves open. Each is built to the recommended default; the alternative is a small change where noted.

1. **The shape of a layout's output.** *Recommended (built):* Linear Pattern is a flat list (paths `{i}`); Grid, Circular and Curve Pattern are always trees of depth 2 (`{row;i}`, `{ring;i}`, `{chain;i}`) and Face Pattern one of depth 3 (`{row;column;0}`, decision 5), even for one row or ring, so adding a ring or a row later never renames the paths (and so the picks) of the existing ones. *Alternative:* a flat list when there is a single branch; cheaper to read, but a pick on `{3}` would become `{0;3}` the day a second ring is added.
2. **Per-ring count of a Circular Pattern.** The spec says "optional rings: ring count, radius step, per-ring count". *Recommended (built):* `countStep`, the number each ring has more than the last (default 0, may be negative). *Alternative:* a list of counts, one per ring; it needs an integer-list source, and no node makes one (Series and Range make numbers, which do not convert to integers), so it could not be wired today. A list-valued `count` can be added with such a node.
3. **How sizes meet a tree of placements.** *Recommended (built, `InstanceGrid`):* the deepest input sets the shape; a flat list applies to every branch and matches its items (as 7a-1's errata word broadcasting); a tree as deep as the shape matches level by level, a shorter side repeating its last branch (so a Graft of `[8, 5]` gives ring 0 Ø8 and ring 1 Ø5); a tree of another depth is **refused** naming both shapes. *Alternative:* broadcasting's rule (match from the outermost level and warn once); it would silently pair unrelated levels.
4. **A tree on the part or the operation** stays refused ("Only placements and sizes can be trees: flatten the tree wired into the part or the operation first."). *Recommended:* refuse; running the node once per branch cuts the part once per branch (7a-2 decision 8a). *Alternative:* run per branch and return a tree of parts, as Boolean does.
5. **Face Pattern's names (what a pick on a vent is stable against).** Spec §3 says a branch per row and §6 says "changing spacing, position or size keeps paths". A path `{row;index}` with `index` counted among the placements *kept* in the row breaks §6: a bigger margin, a bigger hole or a smaller face drops placements and every vent to the right of a dropped one silently takes a new index, so a pick on a vent would name a different physical vent. *Recommended (built):* **(b)** a branch per grid cell inside a branch per row (depth 3, `{row;column;0}`): rows are counted from the lowest row the face's bounding box reaches and columns from the lowest column it reaches (a stagger's half pitch counted in), a cell that is clipped (off the face, inside a hole, within the margin) is an empty branch. A margin edit, a bigger or smaller hole or a hole moved renames nothing, and a pick on a vent that stays stays on it (Tasks 9 and 12 test both). Costs: it is not the spec's literal "one branch per row" (a row is a branch of cells); a Graft of sizes meant to be matched level by level must be as deep (depth 3), while a flat list of sizes still applies to every branch; a face that grows past its box at the left or the bottom renumbers (the numbering starts at the box). *Alternatives:* **(a)** `{row;i}` over the kept placements, the spec's literal shape, with the honest text that a pick on a vent is stable only while the kept placements to its left in that row don't change (Task 9's loop appends only what `region.allows` passes, and `CLAUDE.md`, the Errata and PL-7 say so); **(c)** number each row from its first kept column, which cures a margin eating the left but not a hole in the middle of a row.
6. **Face Pattern's geometry.** The grid is anchored at the face's centroid (symmetric faces get symmetric patterns), a position is kept when it is on the face (even-odd over all loops) and its **point** is at least `margin` from every edge, holes included, by true distance; default margin 3 mm, default spacing 10 × 10; stagger shifts odd rows by half a pitch. It clips the placement's origin, not the tool's footprint. *Alternative:* clip the footprint (needs the tool's size at the layout).
7. **A way to pick the face for Face Pattern.** The spec says "a picked flat face (the same `FacePick` as Plane from Face)" and the only existing UI for a face pick is New Sketch on Face. *Recommended (built, Task 11):* a face-menu entry "Pattern on Face" beside it, inserting a Face Pattern with the pick, wired from the part, as one undo step; refused inside a group (the pick would have to be written relative to the group). *Alternative:* no UI (the node is then only usable from a file or a test), or the unbuilt `pickFacesInView` button. **Optional:** the entry widens the shared-file surface (`CreatorViewport`'s menu files, `CreatorApp`, `UndoName`); Task 11, the Pattern on Face test in Task 13 (`GroupViewportTests`) and the Pattern on Face lines of Task 14 can be cut without touching Tasks 1 to 10 or 12.
8. **Curve Pattern's limits.** Straight and circular edges only (another curve refuses, naming the edge); chains are curves meeting end to end within 0.0001 mm in the order given (a branching set falls into several chains); a closed chain is spaced without repeating its start, an open one from end to end (by count) or from the start (by spacing); a placement exactly where two pieces meet faces along the next one; a profile gives its outline and then each hole as chains. *Alternative:* sample curves through tessellation (any edge, approximate).
9. **Preview details.** Each triad axis is 40% of the smallest distance between neighbouring placements (5 mm for one); a selected layout node is evaluated even in Final preview with nothing wired to it; Face Pattern's dashed outline is the face moved in by the margin with mitred corners (exact on straight edges, a guide at arcs and sharp corners). All of this is a guess for human check PL-3 and PL-6 to settle.
10. **Limits of a path in a tag.** A tree path in a tag has at most 4 indices, each below 32,768 (deeper or wider refuses, naming the instance); flat paths are unlimited (but 2,000 instances). *Alternative:* make `TopoTag.item` a path (a file-format change).
11. **A Boolean fed a tree of copies** takes it a branch at a time (spec §2's rule for `list` sockets): Place over a Grid Pattern into a Boolean's `tools` makes one cut part per row. The shortcuts cut everything at once. *Recommended:* leave it (Flatten first); human check PL-9 records whether it surprises.
12. **A list wired into a layout's single-valued input** (a Series into `spacingX`, `spacing` or `radius`; it can't reach an integer socket such as `countX`, which a number list doesn't convert to). The rest of the app broadcasts a list (the node runs once per value); a layout can't, since it returns one tree. *Recommended (built):* refuse in the layout's own words, before it runs: "Grid Pattern takes single values, not lists: wire one value into each input, or pick one out of the list first with List Item." (`NodeDefinition.singleValuesRefusal`; Task 13 pins the wording for all five). *Alternatives:* leave the evaluator's generic sentence from 7a-1 ("This node works on a whole tree at once, so its other inputs must be single values, not lists."), which fits a layout badly and was the plan's first behaviour; or run once per value and return a deeper tree (a Series of radii gives a ring per radius), a bigger feature that needs the evaluator to join trees.
13. **Previews are drawn through the part.** Spec §8 says the previews are "drawn as guides (like Final preview's selected-rule edges)"; those guides are depth-tested and faded where the part hides them, and carry only selected edges. *Recommended (built):* the sketch editor's overlay (`ViewportOverlay`), drawn over everything with `depthAlways`: it never picks, frames or opens a menu by construction, and the triads read from inside a solid. The cost is that triads at a Hole Pattern's cutting plane inside the plate show through it, never faded. *Alternative:* depth-aware guides, a change to our own viewport renderer (`ViewportRenderer+Guides`), not to MetalUI (no gap is logged for it). Human checks PL-2 and PL-3 look at triads inside and behind the plate and record whether they read or clutter.
14. **`startAngle` on Circular Pattern.** The spec lists sweep and rotate-with-angle only. *Recommended (built):* a `startAngle` (degrees, default 0) so a bolt circle can be turned without turning the part. The acceptance test (`rotatingTheBoltCircleKeepsEveryHolesPath`) and PL-4 use it. *Alternative:* drop it (the socket and its inspector slider in Task 7, that test, the CLAUDE.md and Errata words and the end of PL-4); the circle then always starts at the x axis Plane from Face would give.

## Tasks

### Task 1: Instance paths as tag items

**Files:**
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorKernel/InstancePath.swift`, `Sources/CreatorKernel/Topology+Instances.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorKernel/TopoTag+Item.swift`, `Sources/CreatorGraph/DataTree+Items.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorKernelTests/InstancePathTests.swift` (new), `Tests/CreatorKernelTests/InstanceNamingTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Trees/DataTreeItemsTests.swift` (new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `TopoTag(node:item:role:)`, `EdgeKey`, `Topology.faces[].tags`, `DataTree` (`depth`, `branches`, `itemCount`, `.empty(depth:)`, `.list`, `init?(depth:branches:)`).
- Produces (public): `InstancePath.item(for indices: [Int]) -> Int?` (a one-index path is its own index; 2 to 4 indices, each at most `InstancePath.maximumIndex` = 32,767, are one negative `Int`; anything else `nil`), `InstancePath.indices(ofItem:) -> [Int]?`, `InstancePath.text(indices:) -> String`, `InstancePath.text(_ item: Int) -> String` (now decodes, so every existing caller that prints a tag item prints `{0;3}` for a tree path), `InstancePath.maximumDepth` (4), `InstancePath.maximumIndex`; `Topology.withInstanceItem(_ item: Int) -> Topology`; `DataTree.replacingItems(_ scalars: [Scalar]) -> DataTree?`.

This is the one place a path becomes a number and back. A flat path is its own index on purpose: every pick saved by 7a-2 stored `item: i`, and a flat list is a one-level tree, so `{i}` must equal that form (Task 5 pins it against a saved file).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`Tests/CreatorKernelTests/InstancePathTests.swift`

```swift
import Testing
@testable import CreatorKernel

/// Patterns spec §6: an instance's path is one tag item. A flat path is its index (so picks saved before trees still
/// name the same instance); a deeper one is packed into a negative number and read back.
struct InstancePathTests {
    @Test(arguments: [0, 1, 7, 1_999, 40_000])
    func aFlatPathIsItsOwnIndex(_ index: Int) {
        #expect(InstancePath.item(for: [index]) == index)
        #expect(InstancePath.indices(ofItem: index) == [index])
        #expect(InstancePath.text(index) == "{\(index)}")
    }

    @Test(arguments: [[0, 0], [0, 3], [3, 0], [1, 2, 3], [2, 0, 7, 9], [32_767, 32_767], [32_767, 0, 0, 32_767]])
    func aDeeperPathRoundTripsThroughOneNegativeNumber(_ indices: [Int]) throws {
        let item = try #require(InstancePath.item(for: indices))
        #expect(item < 0)
        #expect(InstancePath.indices(ofItem: item) == indices)
        #expect(InstancePath.text(item) == InstancePath.text(indices: indices))
    }

    @Test func textWritesPathsWithSemicolons() {
        #expect(InstancePath.text(indices: [0, 3]) == "{0;3}")
        #expect(InstancePath.text(InstancePath.item(for: [1, 0, 4]) ?? 0) == "{1;0;4}")
    }

    @Test func noTwoPathsShareAnItem() {
        var seen: [Int: [Int]] = [:]
        for first in 0..<6 {
            for second in 0..<6 {
                for indices in [[first], [first, second], [first, second, 0], [0, first, second]] {
                    let item = InstancePath.item(for: indices)
                    if let item, let other = seen[item] { #expect(other == indices, "\(other) and \(indices) share \(item)") }
                    if let item { seen[item] = indices }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    @Test(arguments: [[], [-1], [0, -1], [0, 32_768], [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]])
    func aPathThatCannotBeNamedGivesNoItem(_ indices: [Int]) {
        #expect(InstancePath.item(for: indices) == nil)
    }

    @Test func anInvalidNegativeNumberIsWrittenAsItIs() {
        #expect(InstancePath.indices(ofItem: -5) == nil)
        #expect(InstancePath.indices(ofItem: Int.min) == nil)
        #expect(InstancePath.text(-5) == "{-5}")
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorKernel/InstancePath.swift`

```swift
/// How a placed copy's position in its pattern is named: in messages (`{3}`, `{0;3}`, patterns spec §2 and §6) and in
/// the `item` of its faces' tags (`TopoTag.item`), which is one `Int`.
///
/// A flat list's instance `i` has the path `{i}` and the item `i`, exactly as before trees, so a pick saved then still
/// names the same instance. A deeper path (`{0;3}`: 2 to 4 indices, each at most `maximumIndex`) is packed into one
/// negative `Int`: the depth in the bits from 60 up, then the indices 15 bits each, the whole negated. No path equals
/// another's, and no deeper path equals a flat one. This is the one place that writes and reads both forms.
public enum InstancePath {
    /// The most indices a path can have and still be named in a tag.
    public static let maximumDepth = 4
    /// The largest index of a path with two or more indices.
    public static let maximumIndex = (1 << indexBits) - 1

    private static let indexBits = 15
    private static let depthShift = 60

    /// The tag item that names the instance at `indices`. `nil` when the path is empty, has a negative index, is deeper
    /// than `maximumDepth`, or (when it has two or more indices) has an index above `maximumIndex`.
    public static func item(for indices: [Int]) -> Int? {
        guard indices.allSatisfy({ $0 >= 0 }) else { return nil }
        if indices.count == 1 { return indices[0] }
        guard (2...maximumDepth).contains(indices.count), indices.allSatisfy({ $0 <= maximumIndex }) else { return nil }
        var packed = indices.count << depthShift
        for (position, index) in indices.enumerated() {
            packed |= index << (indexBits * (indices.count - 1 - position))
        }
        return -packed
    }

    /// The indices a tag item names: `[item]` for a flat instance, the packed path for a negative one, and `nil` for
    /// a negative number that `item(for:)` never made.
    public static func indices(ofItem item: Int) -> [Int]? {
        if item >= 0 { return [item] }
        guard item != Int.min else { return nil }
        let packed = -item
        let depth = packed >> depthShift
        guard (2...maximumDepth).contains(depth) else { return nil }
        let indices = (0..<depth).map { position in (packed >> (indexBits * (depth - 1 - position))) & maximumIndex }
        // Reject a number with stray bits between the indices and the depth.
        return self.item(for: indices) == item ? indices : nil
    }

    /// `{3}` or `{0;3}`: the path of `indices`.
    public static func text(indices: [Int]) -> String {
        "{" + indices.map { String($0) }.joined(separator: ";") + "}"
    }

    /// The path a tag item names, written as in messages. A number that is no path is written as it is.
    public static func text(_ item: Int) -> String {
        text(indices: indices(ofItem: item) ?? [item])
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorKernel/TopoTag+Item.swift`

```swift
extension TopoTag {
    /// This tag with `item` as its item, a blend role's source edge too: a placed copy's faces are first named by the
    /// copy's flat index (`Kernel.place`) and then by its path (`Topology.withInstanceItem(_:)`).
    func withItem(_ item: Int) -> TopoTag {
        TopoTag(node: node, item: item, role: role.withItem(item))
    }
}

extension TopoRole {
    func withItem(_ item: Int) -> TopoRole {
        guard case .blend(let edge) = self else { return self }
        return .blend(sourceEdge: EdgeKey(Set(edge.first.map { $0.withItem(item) }), Set(edge.second.map { $0.withItem(item) })))
    }
}
```

```diff
+    public func withInstanceItem(_ item: Int) -> Topology {
+        var copy = self
+        for index in copy.faces.indices {
+            copy.faces[index].tags = Set(copy.faces[index].tags.map { $0.withItem(item) })
```

`Sources/CreatorGraph/DataTree+Items.swift`

```swift
extension DataTree {
    /// A tree of this shape holding `scalars` instead of its items, in order: the nesting, the empty branches and the
    /// depth stay, so a node can compute one value per item and give them back in the tree it was computed from. `nil`
    /// when `scalars` is not as long as `itemCount`.
    public func replacingItems(_ scalars: [Scalar]) -> DataTree? {
        guard scalars.count == itemCount else { return nil }
        var next = 0
        return rebuilt(from: scalars, next: &next)
    }

    private func rebuilt(from scalars: [Scalar], next: inout Int) -> DataTree {
        if depth == 1 {
            let taken = Array(scalars[next..<next + count])
            next += count
            return .list(taken)
        }
        var rebuiltBranches: [DataTree] = []
        for branch in branches {
            rebuiltBranches.append(branch.rebuilt(from: scalars, next: &next))
        }
        return DataTree(depth: depth, branches: rebuiltBranches) ?? .empty(depth: depth)
    }
}
```

### Task 2: `InstanceGrid`, the one matching rule

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/InstanceGrid.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/InstanceGridTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1 (`InstancePath.item(for:)`, `InstancePath.text`, `DataTree.replacingItems`); `NodeInputs.tree(_:)`; `PatternLimit.check`; `DataTree.leaves`, `.branches`, `.items`, `.shapeText`.
- Produces: `InstanceGrid.init(_ inputs: NodeInputs, _ names: [SocketName]) throws` and `init(_ trees: [(name: SocketName, tree: DataTree)]) throws`; `paths: [[Int]]` (the indices of each instance: `[i]` flat, `[row, i]` in a tree); `items: [Int]` (each instance's tag item); `count`; `tree(of scalars: [Scalar]) -> DataTree` (outputs in the instances' shape); typed per-instance arrays `numbers`, `integers`, `bools`, `planes`, `solids` by socket name. It throws `NodeError.invalidValue` for nesting that doesn't match ("“placements” (1 × 2) and “size” (2 × 1 × 2) are nested differently. Use a flat list, or trees of the same depth."), a branch with nothing to take from ("“size” has no items for the branch {1}."), a path that can't be named, and more than 2,000 instances.

The rule (User decision 3): the deepest input sets the shape, the first asked for winning a tie, and an input with no items at all makes no instances. All flat: the longest sets the count and the shorter repeat their last item, as before trees. Otherwise the shape's items are the instances; a flat list applies to every branch and matches its items; a tree as deep as the shape matches level by level, repeating its last branch.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/InstanceGrid.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// The instances a pattern node makes, and what each one's inputs are (patterns spec §3 and §5, with trees). The
/// inputs are matched by the same rules broadcasting uses, and the node still makes one kernel call for all of them:
///
/// - The deepest input sets the shape: when all are flat, the longest sets the number of instances and a shorter list
///   repeats its last item (as before trees); when one is a tree, its items are the instances and the instance at
///   `{r;c}` is item `c` of branch `r`. Ties go to the first name asked for, which is the placements.
/// - A flat list applies to every branch and matches that branch's items (a flat list of 8 against 3 rows of 8).
/// - A tree as deep as the shape is matched level by level, a shorter side repeating its last branch (a tree of three
///   one-item branches gives each of three rings one value: Graft a list of diameters).
/// - A tree of another depth is refused, naming both shapes, and so is a branch with no items to take from.
///
/// Any input with no items at all makes no instances, as an empty list always has.
struct InstanceGrid {
    /// Each instance's indices in the tree: `[i]` for a flat list, `[r, c]` for item `c` of branch `r`.
    let paths: [[Int]]
    /// Each instance's tag item (`InstancePath.item(for:)`): its flat index for `[i]`, a packed negative number deeper.
    let items: [Int]
    private let shape: DataTree?
    private let depth: Int
    private let columns: [SocketName: [Scalar]]

    var count: Int { paths.count }

    /// The instances of the inputs `names`, each read as a whole tree (`.tree` access, so a flat list arrives as a tree
    /// of depth 1 and a single value as a list of one).
    init(_ inputs: NodeInputs, _ names: [SocketName]) throws {
        try self.init(names.map { (name: $0, tree: try inputs.tree($0)) })
    }

    init(_ trees: [(name: SocketName, tree: DataTree)]) throws {
        guard var lead = trees.first else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("A pattern needs placements.") }
        for entry in trees where entry.tree.depth > lead.tree.depth { lead = entry }
        if trees.contains(where: { $0.tree.itemCount == 0 }) {
            (paths, items, shape, depth, columns) = ([], [], nil, lead.tree.depth, [:])
            return
        }
        var cells: [(path: [Int], leaf: Int, position: Int)] = []
        if lead.tree.depth == 1 {
            let count = trees.map(\.tree.itemCount).max() ?? 0
            try PatternLimit.check(count)
            cells = (0..<count).map { ([$0], 0, $0) }
        } else {
            for entry in trees where entry.tree.depth != 1 && entry.tree.depth != lead.tree.depth {
                throw NodeError.invalidValue("“\(entry.name)” (\(entry.tree.shapeText)) and “\(lead.name)” "
                    + "(\(lead.tree.shapeText)) are nested differently. Use a flat list, or trees of the same depth.")
            }
            try PatternLimit.check(lead.tree.itemCount)
            for (leaf, entry) in lead.tree.leaves.enumerated() {
                for position in entry.items.indices { cells.append((entry.path.indices + [position], leaf, position)) }
            }
        }
        var items: [Int] = []
        items.reserveCapacity(cells.count)
        for cell in cells {
            guard let item = InstancePath.item(for: cell.path) else {
                throw NodeError.invalidValue("Instance \(InstancePath.text(indices: cell.path)) can't be named: patterns take trees up "
                    + "to \(InstancePath.maximumDepth) levels deep, with branch numbers below \((InstancePath.maximumIndex + 1).display).")
            }
            items.append(item)
        }
        var columns: [SocketName: [Scalar]] = [:]
        let leaves = lead.tree.depth == 1 ? [] : lead.tree.leaves
        for entry in trees {
            columns[entry.name] = try Self.values(of: entry, matching: cells.map { ($0.leaf, $0.position, $0.path) }, leaves: leaves)
        }
        (self.paths, self.items, self.depth, self.columns) = (cells.map(\.path), items, lead.tree.depth, columns)
        shape = lead.tree.depth == 1 ? nil : lead.tree
    }

    /// The input's item for each cell: the cell's own position among the items it is matched with, repeating the last.
    private static func values(of entry: (name: SocketName, tree: DataTree), matching cells: [(leaf: Int, position: Int, path: [Int])],
                               leaves: [DataTree.Leaf]) throws -> [Scalar] {
        let tree = entry.tree
        if tree.depth == 1 {
            let items = tree.items
            return cells.map { items[min($0.position, items.count - 1)] }
        }
        var byLeaf: [Int: [Scalar]] = [:]
        var result: [Scalar] = []
        result.reserveCapacity(cells.count)
        for cell in cells {
            if byLeaf[cell.leaf] == nil {
                byLeaf[cell.leaf] = try items(of: entry, at: leaves[cell.leaf].path)
            }
            let items = byLeaf[cell.leaf] ?? []
            result.append(items[min(cell.position, items.count - 1)])
        }
        return result
    }

    /// The items of the branch a leaf is matched with: level by level, a shorter side repeating its last branch.
    private static func items(of entry: (name: SocketName, tree: DataTree), at path: TreePath) throws -> [Scalar] {
        var branch = entry.tree
        for index in path.indices {
            let branches = branch.branches
            guard let last = branches.indices.last else { throw empty(entry.name, path) }
            branch = branches[min(index, last)]
        }
        guard !branch.items.isEmpty else { throw empty(entry.name, path) }
        return branch.items
    }

    private static func empty(_ name: SocketName, _ path: TreePath) -> NodeError {
        NodeError.invalidValue("“\(name)” has no items for the branch \(path).")
    }

    /// A tree holding `scalars`, one per instance, in the shape the instances came in: a flat list for a flat pattern, else
    /// the tree (the empty branches too) the instances sit in.
    func tree(of scalars: [Scalar]) -> DataTree {
        if let shape { return shape.replacingItems(scalars) ?? .empty(depth: depth) }
        return depth > 1 && scalars.isEmpty ? .empty(depth: depth) : .list(scalars)
    }

    // MARK: Typed inputs, one value per instance

    func numbers(_ name: SocketName) throws -> [Double] {
        try typed(name, .number) { if case .number(let value) = $0 { value } else { nil } }
    }

    func integers(_ name: SocketName) throws -> [Int] {
        try typed(name, .integer) { if case .integer(let value) = $0 { value } else { nil } }
    }

    func bools(_ name: SocketName) throws -> [Bool] {
        try typed(name, .bool) { if case .bool(let value) = $0 { value } else { nil } }
    }

    func planes(_ name: SocketName) throws -> [Plane] {
        try typed(name, .plane) { if case .plane(let value) = $0 { value } else { nil } }
    }

    func solids(_ name: SocketName) throws -> [Solid] {
        try typed(name, .solid) { if case .solid(let value) = $0 { value } else { nil } }
    }

    private func typed<T>(_ name: SocketName, _ type: SocketType, _ extract: (Scalar) -> T?) throws -> [T] {
        try (columns[name] ?? []).map { scalar in
            guard let value = extract(scalar) else { throw NodeError.typeMismatch(name, expected: type) }
            return value
        }
    }
}
```

### Task 3: Place takes trees

**Files:**
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PlaceNode.swift`, `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PlacementMoves.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/PlaceTreeTests.swift` (new), `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/PlaceNodeTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1 and 2 (`InstanceGrid`, `Topology.withInstanceItem`); `Kernel.place(_:at:qualifying:tag:)` unchanged.
- Produces: Place's `tool` and `placements` are `.tree` sockets (so the node runs once and keeps the tree shape); `PlacementMoves.place(_ tools:, planes:, items:, placedBy:, kernel:, tag:) async throws -> [Solid]` (one kernel call, then each copy renamed to its item when that is not its flat index); `PlacementMoves.make(_ planes: [Plane], items: [Int]) throws -> [Transform]`. `requireFlat` stays for the shortcuts until Task 4.

The kernel names copy `i` by the flat index `i`; the node then gives each copy `items[i]`. A flat pattern needs no renaming (its items are its indices), so nothing changes for it and Place makes no extra work.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PlaceNode.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// A copy of a tool for every placement (patterns spec §3): the tool is built at the origin facing +Z, and each
/// placement is a plane that says where a copy goes and which way it faces. One kernel call places every copy
/// (`Kernel.place`), and copies of one tool share its geometry. A copy's face tags are its tool's, qualified by the
/// copy's path (`NodeID.instanceScoped`, §6): `{i}` in a flat list, `{row;i}` in a tree, so a pick on one copy follows
/// it through edits to the pattern.
///
/// Tools and placements are matched as `InstanceGrid` says: the longer flat list sets the number of copies and the
/// shorter repeats its last item; a tree of placements (a Grid Pattern's rows, a Circular Pattern's rings) comes out
/// as a tree of copies of the same shape, and a flat tool applies to every branch. Wire the copies into a Boolean's
/// `tools` to cut or join them all at once (it takes one branch of a tree at a time; Flatten them first to use one).
public enum PlaceNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.place"
    public static let displayName = "Place"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.patterns
    public static let placesInstances = true
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("tool", .solid, access: .tree),
        SocketSpec("placements", .plane, access: .tree),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("solids", .solid)]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let grid = try InstanceGrid(inputs, ["placements", "tool"])
        guard grid.count > 0 else { return NodeOutputs(trees: ["solids": grid.tree(of: [])]) }
        let copies = try await PlacementMoves.place(try grid.solids("tool"), planes: try grid.planes("placements"), items: grid.items,
                                                    placedBy: context.node.id, kernel: kernel, tag: context.tag)
        return NodeOutputs(trees: ["solids": grid.tree(of: copies.map(Scalar.solid))])
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PlacementMoves.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// Turns placements into the moves that carry a tool built at the origin, facing +Z, onto them.
enum PlacementMoves {
    /// Refuses a tree on any input of a pattern node (User decision 8a): the node would run once per branch, cutting the
    /// part once for each and restarting `{i}` at 0 in each. Every node that places instances calls this first.
    static func requireFlat(_ context: EvalContext) throws {
        if context.isTreeRun {
            throw NodeError.invalidValue("Patterns take flat lists for now: flatten the tree first.")
        }
    }

    /// The move for each of `count` instances: instance `i` uses placement `i`, and a shorter list repeats its last
    /// placement (broadcasting, spec §4.2). A placement with no usable direction names its path.
    static func make(_ planes: [Plane], count: Int) throws -> [Transform] {
        guard let last = planes.indices.last else { return [] }
        var moves: [Transform] = []
        moves.reserveCapacity(count)
        for index in 0..<count {
            guard let move = planes[min(index, last)].placement else {
                throw NodeError.invalidValue("Placement \(InstancePath.text(index)) has no usable direction: "
                    + "its normal and x axis must not be zero or along each other.")
            }
            moves.append(move)
        }
        return moves
    }

    /// The move for each instance, from the placement `planes[i]` of the instance whose tag item is `items[i]`. A
    /// placement with no usable direction names its path.
    static func make(_ planes: [Plane], items: [Int]) throws -> [Transform] {
        var moves: [Transform] = []
        moves.reserveCapacity(planes.count)
        for (plane, item) in zip(planes, items) {
            guard let move = plane.placement else {
                throw NodeError.invalidValue("Placement \(InstancePath.text(item)) has no usable direction: "
                    + "its normal and x axis must not be zero or along each other.")
            }
            moves.append(move)
        }
        return moves
    }

    /// Every copy of `tools[i]` on `planes[i]`, in one kernel call, named by `items[i]` (`InstancePath.item`): the
    /// kernel names each copy by its flat index, so a copy whose item is another number is renamed here, which changes
    /// no geometry and costs no kernel call.
    static func place(_ tools: [Solid], planes: [Plane], items: [Int], placedBy place: NodeID, kernel: any Kernel,
                      tag: NodeTag) async throws -> [Solid] {
        let moves = try make(planes, items: items)
        let copies = try await kernel.place(tools, at: moves, qualifying: qualifier(placedBy: place), tag: tag)
        return zip(copies, items).enumerated().map { index, pair in
            let (copy, item) = pair
            guard item != index else { return copy }
            return Solid(topology: copy.topology.withInstanceItem(item), bounds: copy.bounds, storage: copy.storage)
        }
    }

    /// How a copy of a tool placed by node `place` names its faces (patterns spec §6): each of the tool's tag nodes
    /// `T` becomes `NodeID.instanceScoped([place, T])`. `place` is the identity the node evaluates under, so a node
    /// inside a group qualifies by its own instance.
    static func qualifier(placedBy place: NodeID) -> @Sendable (NodeID) -> NodeID {
        { NodeID.instanceScoped([place, $0]) }
    }
}
```

### Task 4: The four shortcuts take trees

**Files:**
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/HolePatternNode.swift`, `BossPatternNode.swift`, `SlotPatternNode.swift`, `PatternFeatureNode.swift`, `PatternApply.swift`, `PatternMisses.swift`, `PlacementMoves.swift`
- Delete: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PerInstance.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/PatternTreeShortcutTests.swift` (new), `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/HolePatternNodeTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1 to 3 (`InstanceGrid`, `PlacementMoves.place`).
- Produces: every size socket of the four shortcuts, their `placements` and Pattern Feature's `tool` are `.tree` sockets; `PatternApply.Request` gains `grid: InstanceGrid` (its `tools` and `planes` have one entry per instance); `PatternMisses.count(_:part:result:copies:items:)` counts by tag item; `PlacementMoves.requireSingleValues(_:)` replaces `requireFlat` and refuses a tree on the part or the operation, now checked first in each node, before any tool is built. A bad size names its instance by path ("Hole {1;0}: the diameter must be greater than 0 mm.").

Still one `Kernel.place` and one `Kernel.boolean` for the whole tree. A flat list behaves exactly as before: the longest input sets the count, the shorter repeat their last item.

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import CreatorOCCT
import Testing

/// The shortcut nodes over trees (patterns spec §5 and §6, 7a-3): placements in rows, sizes per instance or per row, still one
/// Place and one Boolean for the whole tree, and every instance named by its path.
struct PatternTreeShortcutTests {
    /// The plate and a 2 × 2 grid of placements cut into rows of 2 (Partition), wired into `node`'s `placements`.
    func rowed(_ node: any NodeDefinition.Type, _ settings: [SocketName: ConstantValue] = [:], rowLength: Int = 2,
               columns: Int = 2, rows: Int = 2) -> (plate: PatternPlate, node: Node) {
        var plate = PatternPlate(columns: columns, rows: rows)
        let shortcut = plate.h.add(node, settings)
        let partition = plate.h.add(PartitionNode.self, ["size": .integer(rowLength)])
        plate.h.wire(plate.plate, "solid", to: shortcut, "part")
        plate.h.wire(plate.placements, "placements", to: partition, "tree")
        plate.h.wire(partition, "tree", to: shortcut, "placements")
        return (plate, shortcut)
    }

    func pathsNamed(in solid: Solid, by node: Node) -> Set<[Int]> {
        let qualifier = NodeID.instanceScoped([node.id, node.id])
        let items = Set(solid.topology.faces.flatMap(\.tags).filter { $0.node == qualifier }.map(\.item))
        return Set(items.compactMap { InstancePath.indices(ofItem: $0) })
    }

    @Test func aTreeOfPlacementsIsOnePlaceAndOneBooleanAndNamesEverySlotByItsPath() async throws {
        let (plate, node) = rowed(SlotPatternNode.self, ["depth": .number(3)])
        let kernel = FakeKernel()

        #expect(await kernel.operationLog.filter { $0 == "place" }.count == 1)
        #expect(await kernel.operationLog.filter { $0 == "boolean" }.count == 1)

        #expect(Set(items.compactMap { InstancePath.indices(ofItem: $0) }) == [[0, 0], [0, 1], [1, 0], [1, 1]])

    @Test func aTreeOnThePartIsRefusedAndNothingIsPlaced() async throws {
        var plate = PatternPlate(columns: 2, rows: 2)

        #expect(await kernel.operationLog.contains("place") == false)
    }

    @Test func aSizeNestedDifferentlyFromThePlacementsNamesBothShapes() async throws {
        let made = rowed(HolePatternNode.self)
        var plate = made.plate
        let node = made.node
        let counts = plate.h.add(SeriesNode.self, ["start": .number(3), "step": .number(1), "count": .integer(8)])
        let halves = plate.h.add(PartitionNode.self, ["size": .integer(4)])
        let quarters = plate.h.add(PartitionNode.self, ["size": .integer(2)])
        plate.h.wire(counts, "values", to: halves, "tree")
        plate.h.wire(halves, "tree", to: quarters, "tree")
        plate.h.wire(quarters, "tree", to: node, "diameter")
        let report = try await plate.h.run([node])
        #expect(report.error(node) == "“placements” (2 × 2) and “diameter” (2 × 2 × 2) are nested differently. "
            + "Use a flat list, or trees of the same depth.")
    }

    @Test func aBadSizeNamesTheInstanceByItsTreePath() async throws {
        let made = rowed(HolePatternNode.self, ["depth": .number(3)])
        var plate = made.plate
        let node = made.node
        let diameters = plate.h.add(SeriesNode.self, ["start": .number(5), "step": .number(-5), "count": .integer(2)])
        let perRow = plate.h.add(GraftNode.self)
        plate.h.wire(diameters, "values", to: perRow, "tree")
        plate.h.wire(perRow, "tree", to: node, "diameter")
        let report = try await plate.h.run([node])
        #expect(report.error(node) == "Hole {1;0}: the diameter must be greater than 0 mm.")
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PlacementMoves.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// Turns placements into the moves that carry a tool built at the origin, facing +Z, onto them.
enum PlacementMoves {
    /// Refuses a tree on the part (or any other single-value input) of a shortcut node (User decision 8a): the node would
    /// run once per branch, cutting the part once for each. Only the placements and the sizes take trees
    /// (`InstanceGrid`), and they are read whole.
    static func requireSingleValues(_ context: EvalContext) throws {
        if context.isTreeRun {
            throw NodeError.invalidValue("Only placements and sizes can be trees: flatten the tree wired into the part "
                + "or the operation first.")
        }
    }

    /// The move for each instance, from the placement `planes[i]` of the instance whose tag item is `items[i]`. A
    /// placement with no usable direction names its path.
    static func make(_ planes: [Plane], items: [Int]) throws -> [Transform] {
        var moves: [Transform] = []
        moves.reserveCapacity(planes.count)
        for (plane, item) in zip(planes, items) {
            guard let move = plane.placement else {
                throw NodeError.invalidValue("Placement \(InstancePath.text(item)) has no usable direction: "
                    + "its normal and x axis must not be zero or along each other.")
            }
            moves.append(move)
        }
        return moves
    }

    /// Every copy of `tools[i]` on `planes[i]`, in one kernel call, named by `items[i]` (`InstancePath.item`): the
    /// kernel names each copy by its flat index, so a copy whose item is another number is renamed here, which changes
    /// no geometry and costs no kernel call.
    static func place(_ tools: [Solid], planes: [Plane], items: [Int], placedBy place: NodeID, kernel: any Kernel,
                      tag: NodeTag) async throws -> [Solid] {
        let moves = try make(planes, items: items)
        let copies = try await kernel.place(tools, at: moves, qualifying: qualifier(placedBy: place), tag: tag)
        return zip(copies, items).enumerated().map { index, pair in
            let (copy, item) = pair
            guard item != index else { return copy }
            return Solid(topology: copy.topology.withInstanceItem(item), bounds: copy.bounds, storage: copy.storage)
        }
    }

    /// How a copy of a tool placed by node `place` names its faces (patterns spec §6): each of the tool's tag nodes
    /// `T` becomes `NodeID.instanceScoped([place, T])`. `place` is the identity the node evaluates under, so a node
    /// inside a group qualifies by its own instance.
    static func qualifier(placedBy place: NodeID) -> @Sendable (NodeID) -> NodeID {
        { NodeID.instanceScoped([place, $0]) }
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PatternApply.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// What a shortcut node does once it has its tools: Place and Boolean underneath (patterns spec §5), as one kernel
/// call each (§7). `Kernel.place` makes every copy at once, and one Boolean cuts or joins all of them as a compound.
enum PatternApply {
    /// The name of one instance of a shortcut in its misses warning: "hole" and "holes".
    struct Nouns {
        let singular: String
        let plural: String
    }

    /// What a shortcut asks for: `operation` of `part` with a copy of `tools[i]` at every instance `i` of `grid`, placed on
    /// `planes[i]` and named by `grid.items[i]`. `tools` and `planes` have one entry per instance.
    struct Request {
        let operation: BooleanOp
        let part: Solid
        let tools: [Solid]
        let planes: [Plane]
        let grid: InstanceGrid
        let nouns: Nouns
    }

    /// Places the copies and combines them with the part. No tools, or no placements, is the part as it is. Warns of
    /// the pieces a result falls into, as Boolean does, and of the instances that missed the part.
    static func run(_ request: Request, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let (op, part, grid) = (request.operation, request.part, request.grid)
        let count = grid.count
        guard count > 0 else { return NodeOutputs(["solid": .solid(part)]) }
        let copies = try await PlacementMoves.place(request.tools, planes: request.planes, items: grid.items,
                                                    placedBy: context.node.id, kernel: kernel, tag: context.tag)
        let result = try await kernel.boolean(op, part, copies, tag: context.tag)
        var warnings = PieceWarning.warnings(for: result)
        let missed = PatternMisses.count(op, part: part, result: result, copies: copies, items: grid.items)
        let nouns = request.nouns
        if let message = PatternMisses.warning(missed: missed, of: count, singular: nouns.singular, plural: nouns.plural) {
            warnings.append(message)
        }
        return NodeOutputs(["solid": .solid(result)], warnings: warnings)
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PatternMisses.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// Which instances of a pattern did nothing to the part (patterns spec §5): a hole in empty space, a boss beside the
/// plate. They don't fail the node; it warns with the count, "3 of 24 holes miss the part."
enum PatternMisses {
    /// How many of `copies` missed `part`; copy `i` is named by the tag item `items[i]` (`InstancePath.item`). A subtracted or intersected copy missed when none of the faces the
    /// operation left carries its tag (a cut leaves its tool's faces as the walls of what it cut, and a tool that
    /// found nothing to cut leaves none). A united copy missed when its bounds don't touch the part's: it brings its
    /// faces along wherever it stands, and a copy apart from the part is a piece of its own.
    static func count(_ op: BooleanOp, part: Solid, result: Solid, copies: [Solid], items: [Int]) -> Int {
        switch op {
        case .union:
            return copies.filter { !touches($0.bounds, part.bounds) }.count
        case .subtract, .intersect:
            let nodes = Set(copies.flatMap { $0.topology.faces.flatMap(\.tags) }.map(\.node).filter(\.isInstanceQualified))
            let present = Set(result.topology.faces.flatMap(\.tags).filter { nodes.contains($0.node) }.map(\.item))
            return items.filter { !present.contains($0) }.count
        }
    }

    /// Whether two boxes overlap or touch, to a micrometre.
    static func touches(_ a: BoundingBox, _ b: BoundingBox) -> Bool {
        let slack = 1e-3
        return a.min.x <= b.max.x + slack && b.min.x <= a.max.x + slack
            && a.min.y <= b.max.y + slack && b.min.y <= a.max.y + slack
            && a.min.z <= b.max.z + slack && b.min.z <= a.max.z + slack
    }

    /// "3 of 24 holes miss the part." (or "1 of 1 hole misses the part."), or `nil` when none missed.
    static func warning(missed: Int, of total: Int, singular: String, plural: String) -> String? {
        guard missed > 0 else { return nil }
        let noun = total == 1 ? "\(singular) misses" : "\(plural) miss"
        return "\(missed.display) of \(total.display) \(noun) the part."
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/HolePatternNode.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// A hole at every placement (patterns spec §5): cut from the placement's origin into the part, against its normal,
/// to a depth or through everything, plain, with a counterbore or with a countersink. It is Place + Boolean
/// underneath: each distinct hole size is built once as a revolved tool and shared by every hole of that size, one
/// kernel call places all the copies, and one subtracts them. A hole whose tool misses the part doesn't fail the
/// node; it warns with the count ("3 of 24 holes miss the part.").
///
/// Every size is a list or a tree, so a hole can differ from the next (`InstanceGrid` matches them to the placements:
/// the longest flat list sets the number of holes and a shorter one repeats its last item; a tree of placements keeps
/// its rows, and a size per row is a Graft of a list). Faces of the hole at path `{i}` or `{row;i}` are tagged with that
/// path, so a fillet on its rim follows it through edits to the count, the spacing or the sizes.
public enum HolePatternNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.holePattern"
    public static let displayName = "Hole Pattern"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.patterns
    public static let placesInstances = true
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("part", .solid),
        SocketSpec("placements", .plane, access: .tree),
        SocketSpec("diameter", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(5), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...100),
        SocketSpec("depth", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(10), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...200),
        SocketSpec("throughAll", .bool, access: .tree, defaultValue: .bool(false)),
        SocketSpec("style", .integer, access: .tree, defaultValue: .integer(0)),
        SocketSpec("counterboreDiameter", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(9), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...200),
        SocketSpec("counterboreDepth", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(3), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...200),
        SocketSpec("countersinkDiameter", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(10), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...200),
        SocketSpec("countersinkAngle", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(90), unit: .degrees, range: 1...179),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("solid", .solid)]
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Hole", controls: [.slider("diameter"), .slider("depth"), .toggle("throughAll", label: "Through all")]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Mouth", controls: [.segmented("style", options: HoleStyle.names)]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Counterbore", controls: [.slider("counterboreDiameter"), .slider("counterboreDepth")]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Countersink", controls: [.slider("countersinkDiameter"), .slider("countersinkAngle")]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        try PlacementMoves.requireSingleValues(context)
        let part = try inputs.solid("part")
        let grid = try InstanceGrid(inputs, ["placements", "diameter", "depth", "throughAll", "style", "counterboreDiameter",
                                             "counterboreDepth", "countersinkDiameter", "countersinkAngle",
        ])
        guard grid.count > 0 else { return NodeOutputs(["solid": .solid(part)]) }
        let planes = try grid.planes("placements")
        let (diameters, depths) = (try grid.numbers("diameter"), try grid.numbers("depth"))
        let (through, styles) = (try grid.bools("throughAll"), try grid.integers("style"))
        let (cbDiameters, cbDepths) = (try grid.numbers("counterboreDiameter"), try grid.numbers("counterboreDepth"))
        let (csDiameters, csAngles) = (try grid.numbers("countersinkDiameter"), try grid.numbers("countersinkAngle"))
        let reach = through.contains(true) ? ThroughReach.length(of: part, placements: planes) : 0
        var sizes: [HoleSize] = []
        for index in 0..<grid.count {
            guard let style = HoleStyle(rawValue: styles[index]) else {
                throw NodeError.invalidValue("Choose \(HoleStyle.names.formatted(.list(type: .or).locale(.messages))) for “style”.")
            }
            let size = HoleSize(diameter: diameters[index], depth: through[index] ? reach : depths[index],
                                style: style, counterbore: (cbDiameters[index], cbDepths[index]),
                                countersink: (csDiameters[index], .degrees(csAngles[index])))
            if let problem = size.problem(at: InstancePath.text(grid.items[index])) { throw NodeError.invalidValue(problem) }
            sizes.append(size)
        }
        let plan = ToolPlan(sizes: sizes)
        var built: [Solid] = []
        for size in plan.distinct {
            let profile = Profile2D.polyline(size.outline, closed: true, plane: .xz)
            built.append(try await kernel.revolve(profile, axis: .z, angle: .degrees(360), tag: context.tag))
        }
        let request = PatternApply.Request(operation: .subtract, part: part, tools: plan.toolIndex.map { built[$0] }, planes: planes,
                                           grid: grid, nouns: PatternApply.Nouns(singular: "hole", plural: "holes"))
        return try await PatternApply.run(request, kernel: kernel, context: context)
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/BossPatternNode.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// A boss or pin at every placement (patterns spec §5): standing on the placement's origin and growing along its
/// normal, with an optional draft (the sides lean in towards the tip) and a rounded tip, unioned onto the part. It is
/// Place + Boolean underneath: each distinct size is built once as a revolved tool (its tip rounded by one Fillet) and
/// shared by every boss of that size, one kernel call places all the copies, and one unions them. A boss that
/// doesn't touch the part doesn't fail the node; it warns with the count ("3 of 24 bosses miss the part.").
///
/// Every size is a list or a tree, so a boss can differ from the next (`InstanceGrid` matches them to the placements, as
/// Hole Pattern's sizes are). Faces of the boss at path `{i}` or `{row;i}` are tagged with that path.
public enum BossPatternNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.bossPattern"
    public static let displayName = "Boss / Pin Pattern"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.patterns
    public static let placesInstances = true
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("part", .solid),
        SocketSpec("placements", .plane, access: .tree),
        SocketSpec("diameter", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(8), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...100),
        SocketSpec("height", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(5), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...200),
        SocketSpec("draft", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(0), unit: .degrees, range: 0...45),
        SocketSpec("tipFillet", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(0), unit: .millimetres, range: 0...20),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("solid", .solid)]
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Boss", controls: [.slider("diameter"), .slider("height")]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Finish", controls: [.slider("draft"), .slider("tipFillet")]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        try PlacementMoves.requireSingleValues(context)
        let part = try inputs.solid("part")
        let grid = try InstanceGrid(inputs, ["placements", "diameter", "height", "draft", "tipFillet"])
        guard grid.count > 0 else { return NodeOutputs(["solid": .solid(part)]) }
        let planes = try grid.planes("placements")
        let (diameters, heights) = (try grid.numbers("diameter"), try grid.numbers("height"))
        let (drafts, fillets) = (try grid.numbers("draft"), try grid.numbers("tipFillet"))
        var sizes: [BossSize] = []
        for index in 0..<grid.count {
            let size = BossSize(diameter: diameters[index], height: heights[index], draft: .degrees(drafts[index]),
                                tipFillet: fillets[index])
            if let problem = size.problem(at: InstancePath.text(grid.items[index])) { throw NodeError.invalidValue(problem) }
            sizes.append(size)
        }
        let plan = ToolPlan(sizes: sizes)
        var built: [Solid] = []
        for size in plan.distinct {
            built.append(try await tool(size, kernel: kernel, tag: context.tag))
        }
        let request = PatternApply.Request(operation: .union, part: part, tools: plan.toolIndex.map { built[$0] }, planes: planes,
                                           grid: grid, nouns: PatternApply.Nouns(singular: "boss", plural: "bosses"))
        return try await PatternApply.run(request, kernel: kernel, context: context)
    }

    /// The boss standing on the origin along +Z: revolved, then its tip rim rounded when `tipFillet` asks.
    private static func tool(_ size: BossSize, kernel: any Kernel, tag: NodeTag) async throws -> Solid {
        let profile = Profile2D.polyline(size.outline, closed: true, plane: .xz)
        let boss = try await kernel.revolve(profile, axis: .z, angle: .degrees(360), tag: tag)
        guard size.tipFillet > 0 else { return boss }
        // The tip's rim is the only circle at the boss's height; the revolve's seams are lines.
        let rim = boss.topology.edges.filter { $0.kind == .circle && abs($0.midpoint.z - size.height) < 1e-6 }
        return try await kernel.fillet(boss, edges: rim.map(\.id), radius: size.tipFillet, tag: tag)
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/SlotPatternNode.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// A slot at every placement (patterns spec §5): rounded ends, `length` long end to end along the placement's X axis
/// and `width` across, cut from the placement's origin into the part, against its normal, to a depth or through
/// everything. It is Place + Boolean underneath: each distinct size is built once as an extruded tool and shared by
/// every slot of that size, one kernel call places all the copies, and one subtracts them. A slot that misses the
/// part doesn't fail the node; it warns with the count ("3 of 24 slots miss the part.").
///
/// Every size is a list or a tree, so a slot can differ from the next (`InstanceGrid` matches them to the placements, as
/// Hole Pattern's sizes are). Faces of the slot at path `{i}` or `{row;i}` are tagged with that path.
public enum SlotPatternNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.slotPattern"
    public static let displayName = "Slot Pattern"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.patterns
    public static let placesInstances = true
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("part", .solid),
        SocketSpec("placements", .plane, access: .tree),
        SocketSpec("length", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(20), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.2...200),
        SocketSpec("width", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(6), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...100),
        SocketSpec("depth", .number, access: .tree, defaultValue: .number(10), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...200),
        SocketSpec("throughAll", .bool, access: .tree, defaultValue: .bool(false)),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("solid", .solid)]
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Slot", controls: [.slider("length"), .slider("width")]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Depth", controls: [.slider("depth"), .toggle("throughAll", label: "Through all")]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        try PlacementMoves.requireSingleValues(context)
        let part = try inputs.solid("part")
        let grid = try InstanceGrid(inputs, ["placements", "length", "width", "depth", "throughAll"])
        guard grid.count > 0 else { return NodeOutputs(["solid": .solid(part)]) }
        let planes = try grid.planes("placements")
        let (lengths, widths) = (try grid.numbers("length"), try grid.numbers("width"))
        let (depths, through) = (try grid.numbers("depth"), try grid.bools("throughAll"))
        let reach = through.contains(true) ? ThroughReach.length(of: part, placements: planes) : 0
        var sizes: [SlotSize] = []
        for index in 0..<grid.count {
            let size = SlotSize(length: lengths[index], width: widths[index], depth: through[index] ? reach : depths[index])
            if let problem = size.problem(at: InstancePath.text(grid.items[index])) { throw NodeError.invalidValue(problem) }
            sizes.append(size)
        }
        let plan = ToolPlan(sizes: sizes)
        // The profile faces down, so extruding it along its normal runs the slot from the origin against +Z.
        let down = Plane(origin: .zero, normal: -.unitZ, xAxis: .unitX)
        var built: [Solid] = []
        for size in plan.distinct {
            built.append(try await kernel.extrude(.slot(length: size.length, width: size.width, plane: down), distance: size.depth,
                                                  mode: .oneSided, tag: context.tag))
        }
        let request = PatternApply.Request(operation: .subtract, part: part, tools: plan.toolIndex.map { built[$0] }, planes: planes,
                                           grid: grid, nouns: PatternApply.Nouns(singular: "slot", plural: "slots"))
        return try await PatternApply.run(request, kernel: kernel, context: context)
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PatternFeatureNode.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// The general pattern shortcut (patterns spec §5): any tool solid, built at the origin facing +Z, unioned onto or
/// subtracted from the part at every placement. It is Place + Boolean underneath: the same as wiring Place's copies
/// into a Boolean's `tools`, in one node, with one kernel call to place the copies and one to combine them.
///
/// Tools and placements are matched as Place's are (`InstanceGrid`), so a list or tree of tools gives each instance its own size. An instance
/// whose tool misses the part doesn't fail the node: it warns with the count ("3 of 24 features miss the part.").
public enum PatternFeatureNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.patternFeature"
    public static let displayName = "Pattern Feature"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.patterns
    public static let placesInstances = true
    public static let operations = ["Union", "Subtract"]
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("part", .solid),
        SocketSpec("tool", .solid, access: .tree),
        SocketSpec("placements", .plane, access: .tree),
        SocketSpec("operation", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(1)),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("solid", .solid)]
    public static let inspector = [InspectorSection(title: "Pattern Feature", controls: [.segmented("operation", options: operations)])]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        try PlacementMoves.requireSingleValues(context)
        let op: BooleanOp = [.union, .subtract][try inputs.choice("operation", options: operations)]
        let part = try inputs.solid("part")
        let grid = try InstanceGrid(inputs, ["placements", "tool"])
        guard grid.count > 0 else { return NodeOutputs(["solid": .solid(part)]) }
        let request = PatternApply.Request(operation: op, part: part, tools: try grid.solids("tool"), planes: try grid.planes("placements"),
                                           grid: grid, nouns: PatternApply.Nouns(singular: "feature", plural: "features"))
        return try await PatternApply.run(request, kernel: kernel, context: context)
    }
}
```

### Task 5: Picks follow tree paths

**Files:**
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorKernel/InstancePath.swift`, `Sources/CreatorKernel/Topology+Instances.swift`, `Sources/CreatorNodes/Selection/EdgeTagMatch.swift`, `Sources/CreatorNodes/Sketch/SketchProjections.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/PatternTreePickTests.swift` (new), `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/Support/LegacyDocuments.swift` (new), `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/InstancePickRenamingTreeTests.swift` (new), `Tests/CreatorKernelTests/InstancePathTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorKernelTests/InstanceNamingTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1 to 4. Everything that reads a tag item already prints it through `InstancePath.text` (Task 1), so Edges by Tag, Plane from Face and a Sketch's projected edge say "Instance {1;1} no longer exists…" with no further change; Group, Ungroup and Make Unique rename nodes, never items.
- Produces: `InstancePath.isOrdered(_:before:)` (paths level by level); `Topology.vanishedInstances(in:)` and the two message builders list several instances in path order ("Instances {0;2} and {1;1} no longer exist…").

This task adds the proofs that 7a-3's naming works end to end: a fillet on the rim at `{1;1}` survives edits to the spacing, diameter, depth and row count; a row longer by one moves the pick to the hole that holds its place with no warning (as 7a-2's decision 7 says for lists); a lower row count warns naming the path; Group, Ungroup and Make Unique keep a tree-path pick working (and undo restores it exactly); and **a pick in 7a-2's flat-index form still resolves after the file is reopened** (the pin 7a-3 requires). The file is not built by today's code: `LegacyDocuments.holePatternPickFormat6` is the text master `9e5d2d5` wrote for a Hole Pattern over a 3 × 2 Points to Placements and an Edges by Tag holding the pick of the rim of the hole at item 4 (captured by running that commit), so the test fails if the stored form of a placed copy's tag ever changes. Task 12 adds the bracket as format 5 wrote it.
