`Tests/CreatorNodesTests/PatternTreePickTests.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import CreatorOCCT
import CreatorSketch
import Foundation
import Testing

/// Patterns spec §6 with trees (7a-3): a pick on the rim of the hole at `{1;1}` follows that path through edits to the
/// pattern, and says so, naming the path, when it is gone. A pick saved before trees (7a-2) still resolves.
/// Which node of the fixture an edit changes.
enum EditTarget { case grid, rows, holes }

@MainActor
struct PatternTreePickTests {
    /// 3 × 2 Ø5 holes 15 mm apart, cut into rows of 3 (Partition), an Edges by Tag on the result and a Fillet on its edges.
    struct Fixture {
        var plate: PatternPlate
        let rows: Node, holes: Node, rule: Node, fillet: Node

        init() {
            plate = PatternPlate(columns: 3, rows: 2, spacingX: 15, spacingY: 15)
            rows = plate.h.add(PartitionNode.self, ["size": .integer(3)])
            holes = plate.h.add(HolePatternNode.self, ["diameter": .number(5), "depth": .number(6)])
            plate.h.wire(plate.plate, "solid", to: holes, "part")
            plate.h.wire(plate.placements, "placements", to: rows, "tree")
            plate.h.wire(rows, "tree", to: holes, "placements")
            rule = plate.h.add(EdgesByTagNode.self)
            plate.h.wire(holes, "solid", to: rule, "solid")
            fillet = plate.h.add(FilletNode.self, ["radius": .number(0.5)])
            plate.h.wire(rule, "edges", to: fillet, "edges")
        }

        /// The tag of the bore of the hole at `path`, the way Hole Pattern names it.
        func bore(_ path: [Int]) -> TopoTag {
            TopoTag(node: NodeID.instanceScoped([holes.id, holes.id]), item: InstancePath.item(for: path) ?? -1, role: .side(segment: 1))
        }

        /// The rim of the hole at `path` on the plate's top.
        func rim(of path: [Int], in solid: Solid) -> EdgeInfo? {
            let top = TopoTag(node: plate.plate.id, item: 0, role: .endCap)
            return solid.topology.edges.first { edge in
                guard edge.kind == .circle, !edge.isSeam, edge.faces.count == 2 else { return false }
                let sides = edge.faces.compactMap { solid.topology.face($0) }
                return sides.contains { $0.tags.contains(bore(path)) } && sides.contains { $0.tags.contains(top) }
            }
        }

        /// Picks the rims of the holes at `paths` on the rule, from a run of the pattern as it is.
        mutating func pick(_ paths: [[Int]], kernel: any Kernel) async throws {
            let solid = try onlySolid(try await plate.h.run([holes], kernel: kernel), holes)
            let edges = try paths.map { try #require(rim(of: $0, in: solid)).id }
            plate.h.set(rule, NodeSetting.picks, .edgePicks(solid.topology.picks(for: edges)))
        }

        func selected(_ report: EvaluationReport) throws -> (solid: Solid, edges: [EdgeInfo]) {
            let set = try #require(report.value(rule, "edges")?.edgeSets?.first)
            return (set.solid, set.edges.compactMap { set.solid.topology.edge($0) })
        }

        func touches(_ path: [Int], _ edge: EdgeInfo, in solid: Solid) -> Bool {
            edge.faces.compactMap { solid.topology.face($0) }.contains { $0.tags.contains(bore(path)) }
        }
    }

    struct Edit: CustomTestStringConvertible {
        let what: String
        let target: EditTarget
        let socket: SocketName
        let value: ConstantValue
        var testDescription: String { what }
    }

    @Test(arguments: [
        Edit(what: "the spacing", target: .grid, socket: "spacingX", value: .number(18)),
        Edit(what: "the diameter", target: .holes, socket: "diameter", value: .number(6)),
        Edit(what: "the depth", target: .holes, socket: "depth", value: .number(4)),
        Edit(what: "the number of rows", target: .grid, socket: "countY", value: .integer(3)),
    ])
    func aFilletOnAnInstancesRimFollowsItsPathThroughAnEditToThePattern(_ edit: Edit) async throws {
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        var f = Fixture()
        try await f.pick([[1, 1]], kernel: kernel)
        let before = try await f.plate.h.run([f.fillet], kernel: kernel)
        #expect(before.isOK(f.rule) && before.isOK(f.fillet), "before changing \(edit.what)")
        let node: Node = switch edit.target {
        case .grid: f.plate.grid
        case .rows: f.rows
        case .holes: f.holes
        }
        f.plate.h.set(node, edit.socket, edit.value)
        let report = try await f.plate.h.run([f.fillet], kernel: kernel)
        #expect(report.isOK(f.rule) && report.isOK(f.fillet), "after changing \(edit.what): \(String(describing: report.state(f.rule)))")
        let (solid, edges) = try f.selected(report)
        #expect(edges.count == 1 && edges.allSatisfy { f.touches([1, 1], $0, in: solid) }, "still the rim of the instance at {1;1}")
    }

    /// A pick names the instance's place in the tree, not the physical hole: rows of 4 make {1;1} the hole that was {1;2},
    /// with no warning.
    @Test func aLongerRowMovesAPickToTheHoleThatHoldsItsPlaceInTheTree() async throws {
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        var f = Fixture()
        try await f.pick([[1, 1]], kernel: kernel)
        func center(_ report: EvaluationReport) throws -> Vector3 {
            let edge = try #require(try f.selected(report).edges.first)
            guard case .circle(let center, _, _, _, _)? = edge.curve else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("not a circle") }
            return center
        }
        let before = try center(try await f.plate.h.run([f.rule], kernel: kernel))
        f.plate.h.set(f.rows, "size", .integer(4))  // 6 points in rows of 4 and 2.
        let report = try await f.plate.h.run([f.rule], kernel: kernel)
        #expect(report.warning(f.rule) == nil)
        #expect(!isClose(try center(report), before), "{1;1} is another hole now")
    }

    @Test func aPickOnAPathThatIsGoneWarnsNamingItAndSelectsNothing() async throws {
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        var f = Fixture()
        try await f.pick([[1, 1]], kernel: kernel)
        f.plate.h.set(f.plate.grid, "countY", .integer(1))  // One row of 3: {0;0} to {0;2}.
        let report = try await f.plate.h.run([f.rule], kernel: kernel)
        #expect(report.warning(f.rule) == "Instance {1;1} no longer exists, so the edge picked on it isn't selected.")
        #expect(try f.selected(report).edges.isEmpty, "never a neighbour's rim")
    }

    @Test func everyVanishedPathIsNamedInPathOrder() async throws {
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        var f = Fixture()
        try await f.pick([[0, 2], [1, 1]], kernel: kernel)
        f.plate.h.set(f.rows, "size", .integer(2))
        f.plate.h.set(f.plate.grid, "countY", .integer(1))  // 3 points in rows of 2 and 1: {0;0}, {0;1}, {1;0}.
        let report = try await f.plate.h.run([f.rule], kernel: kernel)
        #expect(report.warning(f.rule) == "Instances {0;2} and {1;1} no longer exist, so the edges picked on them aren't selected.")
    }

    @Test func aProjectedRimFollowsItsPathAndWarnsNamingItWhenTheHoleIsGone() async throws {
        let kernel = OCCTKernel()
        var f = Fixture()
        let solid = try onlySolid(try await f.plate.h.run([f.holes], kernel: kernel), f.holes)
        let rim = try #require(f.rim(of: [1, 1], in: solid))
        var drawing = Sketch()
        _ = drawing.add(SketchEntity(.projected(ProjectionSource(reference: "p1", curve: .circle(center: .zero, radius: 1)))))
        let pick = ConstantValue.edgePicks(solid.topology.picks(for: [rim.id]))
        let sketch = f.plate.h.add(SketchNode.self, [NodeSetting.sketch: .sketch(drawing), NodeSetting.projection("p1"): pick])
        f.plate.h.wire(f.holes, "solid", to: sketch, "references")
        let before = try await f.plate.h.run([sketch], kernel: kernel)
        #expect(before.warning(sketch)?.contains("no longer exists") != true)
        f.plate.h.set(f.plate.grid, "countY", .integer(1))
        let report = try await f.plate.h.run([sketch], kernel: kernel)
        #expect(report.warning(sketch)?.contains("Projected edge 1 was picked on instance {1;1}, which no longer exists.") == true,
                "\(String(describing: report.warning(sketch)))")
    }

    // MARK: A pick saved in the 7a-2 form

    /// 7a-2 named instance `i` of a flat list `{i}` by the tag item `i` on the node `instanceScoped([placer, tool])`. The file
    /// below is one master 9e5d2d5 wrote (`LegacyDocuments`), not one built by today's code; a flat list is a tree of one level,
    /// so `{4}` must still be the item 4 and the pick must still find the rim of that hole, with no warning.
    @Test func aPickInAFileWrittenBy7a2StillResolvesAfterTheFileIsReopened() async throws {
        #expect(InstancePath.item(for: [4]) == 4)
        let reopened = try DocumentModel(data: Data(LegacyDocuments.holePatternPickFormat6.utf8), registry: BuiltInNodes.registry,
                                         kernel: OCCTKernel())
        let nodes = reopened.graph.nodes.values
        let holes = try #require(nodes.first { $0.typeID == HolePatternNode.typeID })
        let rule = try #require(nodes.first { $0.typeID == EdgesByTagNode.typeID })
        guard case .edgePicks(let picks)? = rule.inputValues[NodeSetting.picks] else {
            Issue.record("the file should hold edge picks")
            return
        }
        let stored = Set(picks.flatMap { $0.key.first.union($0.key.second) })
        let bore = TopoTag(node: NodeID.instanceScoped([holes.id, holes.id]), item: 4, role: .side(segment: 1))
        #expect(stored.contains(bore), "the stored pick names the copy by its flat index, under the same node as today")
        #expect(bore.node.rawValue.uuidString == "88C22DFA-1AF1-8F98-8C92-541F49C0DE7D")

        reopened.previewNode = rule.id
        await reopened.waitForEvaluation()
        guard case .ok? = reopened.results[rule.id]?.state else {
            Issue.record("the old pick should resolve with no warning: \(String(describing: reopened.results[rule.id]?.state))")
            return
        }
        let set = try #require(reopened.results[rule.id]?.outputs?["edges"]?.edgeSets?.first)
        #expect(set.edges.count == 1)
        let edge = try #require(set.edges.first.flatMap { set.solid.topology.edge($0) })
        #expect(edge.faces.compactMap { set.solid.topology.face($0) }.contains { $0.tags.contains(bore) })
    }
}
```

`Tests/CreatorNodesTests/InstancePickRenamingTreeTests.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import Testing
@testable import CreatorOCCT

/// User decision 3 (7a-2) with trees: Group, Ungroup and Make Unique rename a pick on the instance at `{1;1}` exactly as they
/// rename any node's, because they change the identities its tags name and leave the path alone.
@MainActor
struct InstancePickRenamingTreeTests {
    let registry = BuiltInNodes.registry
    let item = InstancePath.item(for: [1, 1])

    func fixture() async throws -> PatternTreePickTests.Fixture {
        var part = PatternTreePickTests.Fixture()
        try await part.pick([[1, 1]], kernel: OCCTKernel())
        return part
    }

    func document(_ part: PatternTreePickTests.Fixture) -> DocumentModel {
        DocumentModel(file: GraphFile(graph: part.plate.h.graph), registry: registry, kernel: OCCTKernel())
    }

    /// The rim items (tag items of the instance faces) the rule at `path` selects in `content`, with its state.
    func selection(_ content: GraphContent, rule path: [NodeID]) async throws -> (state: NodeState?, items: [Int]) {
        let top = try #require(path.first)
        let report = try await Evaluator(registry: registry, kernel: OCCTKernel())
            .evaluate(content.graph, definitions: content.definitions, demand: Set(content.graph.nodes.keys))
        let result = path.count == 1 ? report.results[top] : report.innerResults[path]
        guard let set = result?.outputs?["edges"]?.edgeSets?.first else { return (result?.state, []) }
        let items = set.edges.flatMap { id -> [Int] in
            guard let edge = set.solid.topology.edge(id) else { return [] }
            return edge.faces.compactMap { set.solid.topology.face($0) }.flatMap(\.tags)
                .filter { $0.node.isInstanceQualified }.map(\.item)
        }
        return (result?.state, items)
    }

    func expectRim(_ content: GraphContent, rule path: [NodeID], sourceLocation: SourceLocation = #_sourceLocation) async throws {
        let (state, items) = try await selection(content, rule: path)
        guard case .ok? = state else {
            Issue.record("the pick should resolve with no warning, got \(String(describing: state))", sourceLocation: sourceLocation)
            return
        }
        #expect(items == [item].compactMap { $0 }, "the rim of instance {1;1}", sourceLocation: sourceLocation)
    }

    @Test func groupingAPatternNodeRenamesATreePathPickAndUndoRestoresIt() async throws {
        let part = try await fixture()
        let document = document(part)
        let before = document.content
        try await expectRim(before, rule: [part.rule.id])
        let edit = try GroupCommands.group([part.holes.id], in: .root, of: document.content, registry: registry)
        try document.perform(edit.command)
        #expect(document.content.graph.nodes[part.rule.id]?.inputValues[NodeSetting.picks]
            != before.graph.nodes[part.rule.id]?.inputValues[NodeSetting.picks], "the pick was renamed")
        try await expectRim(document.content, rule: [part.rule.id])
        document.undo()
        #expect(document.content.graph.nodes == before.graph.nodes, "undo restores every setting, the pick exactly")
    }

    @Test func ungroupingRenamesATreePathPickInsideTheGroupToTheSplicedPatternNode() async throws {
        let part = try await fixture()
        let document = document(part)
        let grouped = try GroupCommands.group([part.holes.id, part.rule.id], in: .root, of: document.content, registry: registry)
        try document.perform(grouped.command)
        let group = try #require(grouped.selection.first)
        try await expectRim(document.content, rule: [group, part.rule.id])
        let before = document.content

        let edit = try GroupCommands.ungroup(group, in: .root, of: document.content, registry: registry)
        try document.perform(edit.command)
        let rule = try #require(document.content.graph.nodes.values.first { $0.typeID == EdgesByTagNode.typeID })
        try await expectRim(document.content, rule: [rule.id])
        document.undo()
        #expect(document.content == before)
    }

    @Test func makeUniqueGivesTheCopyTreePathPicksThatNameItsOwnInstanceFaces() async throws {
        let part = try await fixture()
        let document = document(part)
        let ids = Set(part.plate.h.nodes.keys).subtracting([part.plate.grid.id, part.plate.placements.id, part.rows.id, part.fillet.id])
        let grouped = try GroupCommands.group(ids, in: .root, of: document.content, registry: registry)
        try document.perform(grouped.command)
        let group = try #require(grouped.selection.first)
        try await expectRim(document.content, rule: [group, part.rule.id])
        let before = document.content

        let edit = try GroupCommands.makeUnique(group, in: .root, of: document.content, registry: registry)
        try document.perform(edit.command)
        let copy = try #require(document.content.graph.nodes[group]?.inputValues[NodeSetting.group]?.groupID)
        let rule = try #require(document.content.definitions[copy]?.graph.nodes.values.first { $0.typeID == EdgesByTagNode.typeID })
        try await expectRim(document.content, rule: [group, rule.id])
        document.undo()
        #expect(document.content == before)
    }

    @Test func aTreePathPickOnAnInstanceThatIsGoneStillWarnsAfterGrouping() async throws {
        var part = try await fixture()
        part.plate.h.set(part.plate.grid, "countY", .integer(1))  // One row: {1;1} is gone.
        let document = document(part)
        let message = "Instance {1;1} no longer exists, so the edge picked on it isn't selected."
        let (beforeState, beforeItems) = try await selection(document.content, rule: [part.rule.id])
        #expect(beforeState == .warning(message) && beforeItems.isEmpty)
        let edit = try GroupCommands.group([part.holes.id], in: .root, of: document.content, registry: registry)
        try document.perform(edit.command)
        let (state, items) = try await selection(document.content, rule: [part.rule.id])
        #expect(state == .warning(message), "renaming never invents a target")
        #expect(items.isEmpty)
    }
}
```

### Task 6: Linear Pattern and Grid Pattern

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/LayoutChecks.swift`, `LinearPatternNode.swift`, `GridPatternNode.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/NodeDefinition.swift`, `Sources/CreatorGraph/Evaluator+Running.swift`, `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/PatternLimit.swift`, `Sources/CreatorNodes/BuiltInNodes.swift`, `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/OutputNodeTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorEditorTests/PatternsLibraryTests.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/LinearPatternNodeTests.swift`, `GridPatternNodeTests.swift` (new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `FacePlane.plane(origin:normal:)` (the x axis Plane from Face gives), `PatternLimit`, `NodeOutputs(lists:)` and `NodeOutputs(trees:)`, `DataTree(depth:branches:)`.
- Produces: node types `creator.linearPattern` ("Linear Pattern": start, direction, count, spacing, optional length, facing; output `placements`, a flat list) and `creator.gridPattern` ("Grid Pattern": plane, countX, countY, spacingX, spacingY, centred, stagger; output `placements`, a tree with one branch per row, depth 2 even for one row); `PatternLimit.refusal`; `LayoutChecks.count(_:_:)`, `.product(_:_:)`, `.number(_:_:atLeast:)`, `.direction(_:_:)`, `.placement(_:normal:xAxis:)`, `.singleValues(_:)`; `NodeDefinition.singleValuesRefusal` (default `nil`; every layout sets it, User decision 12). Both nodes sit in the Patterns category before Place.

Linear and Grid are single-valued: a list wired into a Grid Pattern's counts would make the node run once per value, which a node that returns a tree can't (the evaluator's rule, whose generic sentence reads badly here). So `Evaluator.run` stops first, before running the node, and says what the node's `singleValuesRefusal` says ("Grid Pattern takes single values, not lists: wire one value into each input, or pick one out of the list first with List Item."); a node without one broadcasts as always. Task 13 pins the wording for all five layouts. Centred means the middle of the whole grid, with a stagger's half pitch counted in the width.

```swift
/// Linear Pattern (patterns spec §3): placements along a line, one branch.
struct LinearPatternNodeTests {
    func run(_ settings: [SocketName: ConstantValue] = [:]) async throws -> (report: EvaluationReport, node: Node) {
        var h = Harness()
        let node = h.add(LinearPatternNode.self, settings)
        return (try await h.run([node]), node)
    }

    func planes(_ settings: [SocketName: ConstantValue]) async throws -> [Plane] {
        let (report, node) = try await run(settings)
        return try #require(report.value(node, "placements")?.planes, "\(String(describing: report.state(node)))")
    }

    @Test func fourPlacementsTenApartFromTheStartAlongTheDirection() async throws {
        let planes = try await planes(["start": .vector(Vector3(1, 2, 3)), "direction": .vector(.unitY), "count": .integer(4),
                                       "spacing": .number(10),
        ])

        #expect(planes.allSatisfy { $0.normal == .unitZ && $0.xAxis == .unitX })
    }

    @Test func theOutputIsAFlatListSoPlacesNameItsInstancesByIndex() async throws {
        let (report, node) = try await run()
        #expect(report.value(node, "placements")?.isList == true)
        #expect(report.isOK(node))
    }

    @Test func aTotalLengthSpreadsThePlacementsOverIt() async throws {
        let planes = try await planes(["count": .integer(4), "length": .number(30), "spacing": .number(99)])
        #expect(planes.map(\.origin.x) == [0, 10, 20, 30])
    }

    @Test func aSinglePlacementStaysAtTheStartWhateverTheLength() async throws {
        let planes = try await planes(["count": .integer(1), "length": .number(30)])
        #expect(planes.map(\.origin) == [.zero])

    @Test func aSlantedDirectionIsNormalisedSoSpacingIsTheRealDistance() async throws {
```

```swift
/// Grid Pattern (patterns spec §3): placements on a grid in a plane, one branch per row.
struct GridPatternNodeTests {

    }

    }

        #expect(rows.map { $0.map(\.origin.y) } == [[0, 0, 0], [10, 10, 10]])
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/LayoutChecks.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph

/// The checks every layout node makes of its inputs, so each refuses a bad one in the same words (patterns spec §3).
enum LayoutChecks {
    /// What a layout says when a list is wired into one of its single-valued inputs (`NodeDefinition.singleValuesRefusal`): a
    /// layout makes one tree of placements, it does not run once per value.
    static func singleValues(_ node: String) -> String {
        "\(node) takes single values, not lists: wire one value into each input, or pick one out of the list first with List Item."
    }

    /// A count that isn't negative.
    static func count(_ value: Int, _ name: SocketName) throws -> Int {
        guard value >= 0 else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("“\(name)” can't be negative.") }
        return value
    }

    /// `a × b` instances, refused over the pattern limit before any of them is made (and without overflowing). Each count is
    /// also checked on its own: a zero count makes the product 0, but the other count would still size a loop of empty rows.
    static func product(_ a: Int, _ b: Int) throws -> Int {
        try PatternLimit.check(a)
        try PatternLimit.check(b)
        let (total, overflowed) = a.multipliedReportingOverflow(by: b)
        guard !overflowed else { throw PatternLimit.refusal }
        try PatternLimit.check(total)
        return total
    }

    /// A number that is finite and at least `minimum`.
    static func number(_ value: Double, _ name: SocketName, atLeast minimum: Double = 0) throws -> Double {
        guard value.isFinite, value >= minimum else {
            throw NodeError.invalidValue("“\(name)” must be \(minimum == 0 ? "0 or more" : "at least \(minimum.display)").")
        }
        return value
    }

    /// A direction with a length, as a unit vector.
    static func direction(_ vector: Vector3, _ name: SocketName) throws -> Vector3 {
        guard vector.isFinite, let unit = vector.normalized else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("“\(name)” can't be zero.") }
        return unit
    }

    /// The placement at `origin` facing `normal` (unit) with x axis `xAxis`.
    static func placement(_ origin: Vector3, normal: Vector3, xAxis: Vector3) -> Scalar {
        .plane(Plane(origin: origin, normal: normal, xAxis: xAxis))
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/LinearPatternNode.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// Placements along a line (patterns spec §3): `count` of them from `start` along `direction`, `spacing` apart, or
/// spread over a total `length` when that is set (the last at `start + length`; one placement stays at `start`). Each
/// faces `facing`, with the x axis Plane from Face would give (`FacePlane`). One branch: a flat list.
///
/// More than 2,000 refuses ("Patterns are limited to 2,000 instances.").
public enum LinearPatternNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.linearPattern"
    public static let displayName = "Linear Pattern"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.patterns
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("start", .vector, defaultValue: .vector(.zero)),
        SocketSpec("direction", .vector, defaultValue: .vector(.unitX)),
        SocketSpec("count", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(4), unit: .count),
        SocketSpec("spacing", .number, defaultValue: .number(10), unit: .millimetres, range: 0...200),
        SocketSpec("length", .number, unit: .millimetres, range: 0...1_000, optional: true),
        SocketSpec("facing", .vector, defaultValue: .vector(.unitZ)),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("placements", .plane)]
    public static var singleValuesRefusal: String? { LayoutChecks.singleValues(displayName) }
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Row", controls: [.vector("start"), .vector("direction"), .integer("count")]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Spacing", controls: [.slider("spacing"), .number("length")]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Facing", controls: [.vector("facing")]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let count = try LayoutChecks.count(try inputs.integer("count"), "count")
        try PatternLimit.check(count)
        let direction = try LayoutChecks.direction(try inputs.vector("direction"), "direction")
        let facing = try LayoutChecks.direction(try inputs.vector("facing"), "facing")
        let start = try inputs.vector("start")
        let step: Double
        if inputs.has("length") {
            let length = try LayoutChecks.number(try inputs.number("length"), "length")
            step = count > 1 ? length / Double(count - 1) : 0
        } else {
            step = try LayoutChecks.number(try inputs.number("spacing"), "spacing")
        }
        let planes = (0..<count).map { index in
            Scalar.plane(FacePlane.plane(origin: start + direction * (Double(index) * step), normal: facing))
        }
        return NodeOutputs(lists: ["placements": planes])
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/GridPatternNode.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// Placements on a grid in a plane (patterns spec §3): `countX` columns along the plane's x axis and `countY` rows along
/// its y axis, `spacingX` and `spacingY` apart, each facing along the plane's normal with the plane's x axis. `centred`
/// puts the middle of the whole grid on the plane's origin; otherwise the first placement is there. `stagger` moves every
/// second row half a pitch along x (hex-style), and the grid is centred with that shift in it. One branch per row, so a
/// placement is `{row;column}` and always a tree, even for one row (a row added later changes no other path).
///
/// More than 2,000 refuses ("Patterns are limited to 2,000 instances.").
public enum GridPatternNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.gridPattern"
    public static let displayName = "Grid Pattern"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.patterns
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("plane", .plane, defaultValue: .plane(.xy)),
        SocketSpec("countX", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(4), unit: .count),
        SocketSpec("countY", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(3), unit: .count),
        SocketSpec("spacingX", .number, defaultValue: .number(10), unit: .millimetres, range: 0...200),
        SocketSpec("spacingY", .number, defaultValue: .number(10), unit: .millimetres, range: 0...200),
        SocketSpec("centred", .bool, defaultValue: .bool(true)),
        SocketSpec("stagger", .bool, defaultValue: .bool(false)),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("placements", .plane)]
    public static var singleValuesRefusal: String? { LayoutChecks.singleValues(displayName) }
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Grid", controls: [.planePicker("plane"), .integer("countX"), .integer("countY")]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Spacing", controls: [.slider("spacingX"), .slider("spacingY"),
                                                      .toggle("centred", label: "Centred"), .toggle("stagger", label: "Stagger"),
        ]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let plane = try inputs.plane("plane")
        guard plane.placement != nil else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("The grid's plane has no usable direction.") }
        let columns = try LayoutChecks.count(try inputs.integer("countX"), "countX")
        let rows = try LayoutChecks.count(try inputs.integer("countY"), "countY")
        _ = try LayoutChecks.product(columns, rows)
        let spacing = Vector2(try LayoutChecks.number(try inputs.number("spacingX"), "spacingX"),
                              try LayoutChecks.number(try inputs.number("spacingY"), "spacingY"))
        let (centred, stagger) = (try inputs.bool("centred"), try inputs.bool("stagger"))
        // The shift every second row gets, and the width and height of the grid, so a centred grid is centred with it.
        let shift = stagger ? spacing.x / 2 : 0
        let width = Double(max(columns - 1, 0)) * spacing.x + (rows > 1 ? shift : 0)
        let height = Double(max(rows - 1, 0)) * spacing.y
        let corner = centred ? Vector2(-width / 2, -height / 2) : .zero
        var branches: [DataTree] = []
        for row in 0..<rows {
            let offset = row % 2 == 1 ? shift : 0
            let placements = (0..<columns).map { column -> Scalar in
                let local = corner + Vector2(Double(column) * spacing.x + offset, Double(row) * spacing.y)
                return LayoutChecks.placement(plane.point(local), normal: plane.normal, xAxis: plane.xAxis)
            }
            branches.append(.list(placements))
        }
        return NodeOutputs(trees: ["placements": DataTree(depth: 2, branches: branches) ?? .empty(depth: 2)])
    }
}
```

### Task 7: Circular Pattern

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/CircularPatternNode.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorNodes/BuiltInNodes.swift`, `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/OutputNodeTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorEditorTests/PatternsLibraryTests.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/CircularPatternNodeTests.swift` (new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 6 (`LayoutChecks`, `PatternLimit`), `FacePlane`.
- Produces: node type `creator.circularPattern` ("Circular Pattern": centre, axis, radius, count, sweep, startAngle, rotate, rings, radiusStep, countStep; output `placements`, a tree with one branch per ring).

A full sweep (360° or more) spaces `count` evenly without repeating the first; a smaller one puts the last at the end of the sweep (one placement stays at the start). Ring `k` has radius `radius + k × radiusStep` and `count + k × countStep` placements; the total is checked against 2,000 before anything is made, without overflowing on `Int.max`. With `rotate` each x axis points outwards. The angle is measured from the x axis Plane from Face gives for the axis (world X projected, else world Y).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/CircularPatternNode.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Placements on a circle, or on several (patterns spec §3): `count` of them about `centre`, turning about `axis`, at
/// `radius`, over a `sweep` angle from `startAngle` (measured from the direction Plane from Face would give an x axis for
/// that axis). A full sweep of 360° spaces them evenly without repeating the first; a smaller one puts the last at the end
/// of the sweep. Each faces along the axis; with `rotate` its x axis points outwards from the centre, so a slot in it runs
/// along the radius, and without it every x axis is the same.
///
/// `rings` makes concentric rings: ring `k` has radius `radius + k × radiusStep` and `count + k × countStep` placements
/// (a step of 6 makes a ring of 6 inside one of 12). One branch per ring, so a placement is `{ring;index}`: always a tree,
/// even for one ring, so adding a ring renames no other path.
///
/// More than 2,000 placements in all refuses ("Patterns are limited to 2,000 instances.").
public enum CircularPatternNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.circularPattern"
    public static let displayName = "Circular Pattern"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.patterns
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("centre", .vector, defaultValue: .vector(.zero)),
        SocketSpec("axis", .vector, defaultValue: .vector(.unitZ)),
        SocketSpec("radius", .number, defaultValue: .number(20), unit: .millimetres, range: 0...200),
        SocketSpec("count", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(6), unit: .count),
        SocketSpec("sweep", .number, defaultValue: .number(360), unit: .degrees, range: -360...360),
        SocketSpec("startAngle", .number, defaultValue: .number(0), unit: .degrees, range: -360...360),
        SocketSpec("rotate", .bool, defaultValue: .bool(true)),
        SocketSpec("rings", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(1), unit: .count),
        SocketSpec("radiusStep", .number, defaultValue: .number(10), unit: .millimetres, range: -100...100),
        SocketSpec("countStep", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(0), unit: .count),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("placements", .plane)]
    public static var singleValuesRefusal: String? { LayoutChecks.singleValues(displayName) }
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Circle", controls: [.vector("centre"), .vector("axis"), .slider("radius"), .integer("count")]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Sweep", controls: [
            .slider("sweep"), .slider("startAngle"), .toggle("rotate", label: "Rotate with angle"),
        ]),
        InspectorSection(title: "Rings", controls: [.integer("rings"), .slider("radiusStep"), .integer("countStep")]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let centre = try inputs.vector("centre")
        let axis = try LayoutChecks.direction(try inputs.vector("axis"), "axis")
        let radius = try inputs.number("radius")
        let (sweep, start) = (try inputs.number("sweep"), try inputs.number("startAngle"))
        guard sweep.isFinite, start.isFinite, radius.isFinite else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("The circle's numbers must be finite.") }
        let (step, rotate) = (try inputs.number("radiusStep"), try inputs.bool("rotate"))
        let rings = try LayoutChecks.count(try inputs.integer("rings"), "rings")
        try PatternLimit.check(rings)
        let count = try LayoutChecks.count(try inputs.integer("count"), "count")
        let countStep = try inputs.integer("countStep")
        // Each ring's count is checked against the limit before it joins the total, so nothing is made for a pattern that's too big.
        var ringCounts: [Int] = []
        var total = 0
        for ring in 0..<rings {
            let (stepped, overflowed) = ring.multipliedReportingOverflow(by: countStep)
            let (ringCount, added) = count.addingReportingOverflow(stepped)
            guard !overflowed, !added else { throw PatternLimit.refusal }
            guard ringCount >= 0 else {
                throw NodeError.invalidValue("Ring \(ring) would have a negative count: raise “count” or lower “countStep”.")
            }
            try PatternLimit.check(ringCount)
            total += ringCount
            try PatternLimit.check(total)
            ringCounts.append(ringCount)
        }
        let around = FacePlane.plane(origin: centre, normal: axis).xAxis
        let across = axis.cross(around)
        let isFull = abs(sweep) >= 360 - 1e-9
        var branches: [DataTree] = []
        for (ring, ringCount) in ringCounts.enumerated() {
            let ringRadius = radius + Double(ring) * step
            guard ringRadius >= 0 else {
                throw NodeError.invalidValue("Ring \(ring) would have a negative radius: raise “radius” or lower “radiusStep”.")
            }
            let angleStep = isFull ? sweep / Double(max(ringCount, 1)) : (ringCount > 1 ? sweep / Double(ringCount - 1) : 0)
            let placements = (0..<ringCount).map { index -> Scalar in
                let angle = (start + angleStep * Double(index)) * .pi / 180
                let outwards = around * cos(angle) + across * sin(angle)
                return LayoutChecks.placement(centre + outwards * ringRadius, normal: axis, xAxis: rotate ? outwards : around)
            }
            branches.append(.list(placements))
        }
        return NodeOutputs(trees: ["placements": DataTree(depth: 2, branches: branches) ?? .empty(depth: 2)])
    }
}
```

```diff
         FilletNode.self, ChamferNode.self,
```

### Task 8: Curve Pattern

**Files:**
- Create: `Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/CurveChain.swift`, `EdgeCurve+Path.swift`, `EdgeChains.swift`, `CurvePatternNode.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorNodes/BuiltInNodes.swift`, `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/OutputNodeTests.swift`, `Tests/CreatorEditorTests/PatternsLibraryTests.swift`
- Test: `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/EdgeChainsTests.swift`, `CurvePatternNodeTests.swift` (new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `EdgeCurve` (`.line`, `.circle`, `EdgeInfo.curve`), `EdgeSet`, `Profile2D.loops`, `Plane.point`, `FacePlane`, `LayoutChecks`.
- Produces: `CurveChain(_ pieces: [EdgeCurve], isClosed: Bool)` with `length`, `sample(at:) -> (point, tangent)?`, `distances(count:)`, `count(spacing:)`, `distances(spacing:)`; `EdgeCurve` extensions (`pathLength`, `startPoint`, `endPoint`, `reversed`, `isFullCircle`, `sample(at:)`); `EdgeChains.chains(of: [EdgeCurve]) -> [CurveChain]` and `chains(of: Profile2D)`; node type `creator.curvePattern` ("Curve Pattern": `edges` (an edge-set list) or `profile` (a list), `mode` 0 = by count / 1 = by spacing, `count`, `spacing`; output `placements`, a tree with one branch per chain). Also used by Task 9 (`EdgeChains`, `EdgeCurve.sample`).

Each placement faces along the curve's direction of travel. Both inputs are optional and exactly one must be wired. A node returning a tree can't broadcast, so `edges` and `profile` are `.list` sockets (all sets / profiles in one go).

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorNodes

/// Edge chains (Curve Pattern, patterns spec §3): curves that meet end to end are one chain, from one free end to the other.
struct EdgeChainsTests {
    func line(_ a: Vector3, _ b: Vector3) -> EdgeCurve { .line(start: a, end: b) }

    let a = Vector3(0, 0, 0), b = Vector3(10, 0, 0), c = Vector3(10, 10, 0), d = Vector3(0, 10, 0)

    @Test func shuffledAndReversedEdgesJoinIntoOneOrderedOpenChain() throws {
        let chains = EdgeChains.chains(of: [line(c, b), line(a, b), line(c, d)])
        let chain = try #require(chains.first)
        #expect(chains.count == 1 && !chain.isClosed)
        #expect(chain.pieces.map(\.startPoint) == [a, b, c])
        #expect(chain.pieces.map(\.endPoint) == [b, c, d])
        #expect(isClose(chain.length, 30))
    }

    @Test func edgesThatEndWhereTheyBeganAreAClosedChain() throws {
        let chains = EdgeChains.chains(of: [line(a, b), line(c, b), line(c, d), line(a, d)])
        let chain = try #require(chains.first)
        #expect(chains.count == 1 && chain.isClosed && chain.pieces.count == 4)
        #expect(isClose(chain.length, 40))
    }

    @Test func separateEdgesAreSeparateChainsInTheOrderGiven() {
        let far = Vector3(100, 0, 0)
        let chains = EdgeChains.chains(of: [line(a, b), line(far, far + b), line(c, d)])
        #expect(chains.map(\.pieces.count) == [1, 1, 1])
        #expect(chains.map { $0.pieces[0].startPoint } == [a, far, c])
    }

    @Test func aFullCircleIsAChainOfItsOwnBesideALine() throws {
        let circle = EdgeCurve.circle(center: .zero, axis: .unitZ, radius: 5, start: Vector3(5, 0, 0), sweep: 2 * .pi)
        let chains = EdgeChains.chains(of: [line(a, b), circle])
        #expect(chains.count == 2 && chains[1].isClosed && chains[0].isClosed == false)
        #expect(isClose(chains[1].length, 10 * .pi))
    }

    @Test func aBranchingSetFallsIntoSeveralChainsWithNoEdgeLost() {
        let hub = Vector3(0, 0, 0)
        let arms = [line(hub, Vector3(10, 0, 0)), line(hub, Vector3(0, 10, 0)), line(hub, Vector3(-10, 0, 0))]
        let chains = EdgeChains.chains(of: arms)
        #expect(chains.map(\.pieces.count).reduce(0, +) == 3)
        #expect(chains.count == 2)
    }

    @Test func anArcContinuesALineAndIsSampledAlongItsLength() throws {
        // A quarter circle of radius 10 about the origin from (10, 0) to (0, 10), then a line from there to (0, 20): the chain starts
        // at the first curve with a free end, so the arc given first sets the way it runs.
        let arc = EdgeCurve.circle(center: .zero, axis: .unitZ, radius: 10, start: Vector3(10, 0, 0), sweep: .pi / 2)
        let chain = try #require(EdgeChains.chains(of: [arc, line(Vector3(0, 10, 0), Vector3(0, 20, 0))]).first)
        #expect(chain.pieces.count == 2 && isClose(chain.length, 5 * .pi + 10))
        let half = try #require(chain.sample(at: 2.5 * .pi))
        #expect(isClose(half.point, Vector3(10 * cos(.pi / 4), 10 * sin(.pi / 4), 0)))
        #expect(isClose(half.tangent, Vector3(-sin(.pi / 4), cos(.pi / 4), 0)))
        let onLine = try #require(chain.sample(at: 5 * .pi + 4))
        #expect(isClose(onLine.point, Vector3(0, 14, 0)) && isClose(onLine.tangent, .unitY))
    }

    @Test func aClockwiseArcRunsTheOtherWay() throws {
        let arc = EdgeCurve.circle(center: .zero, axis: .unitZ, radius: 10, start: Vector3(10, 0, 0), sweep: -.pi / 2)
        let end = try #require(arc.sample(at: arc.pathLength))
        #expect(isClose(end.point, Vector3(0, -10, 0)) && isClose(end.tangent, -.unitX))
        #expect(isClose(arc.endPoint, Vector3(0, -10, 0)))
        #expect(isClose(arc.reversed.startPoint, Vector3(0, -10, 0)) && isClose(arc.reversed.endPoint, Vector3(10, 0, 0)))
    }

    @Test func countsAndSpacingsFollowTheirRules() {
        let open = CurveChain([line(a, Vector3(60, 0, 0))], isClosed: false)
        #expect(open.distances(count: 4) == [0, 20, 40, 60])
        #expect(open.distances(count: 1) == [0] && open.distances(count: 0).isEmpty)
        #expect(open.count(spacing: 20) == 4 && open.count(spacing: 25) == 3 && open.count(spacing: 70) == 1)
        let closed = CurveChain([line(a, Vector3(60, 0, 0))], isClosed: true)
        #expect(closed.distances(count: 4) == [0, 15, 30, 45])
        #expect(closed.count(spacing: 20) == 3 && closed.count(spacing: 70) == 1)
        #expect(open.count(spacing: 0) == 0)
    }

    @Test func aProfileGivesItsOutlineThenItsHolesAsChains() throws {
        let outer = Profile2D.rectangle(width: 40, height: 20, plane: .xy).outer
        let hole = Profile2D.circle(radius: 3, center: .zero, plane: .xy).outer
        let chains = EdgeChains.chains(of: Profile2D(plane: .xy, outer: outer, holes: [hole]))
        #expect(chains.count == 2 && chains.allSatisfy(\.isClosed))
        #expect(isClose(chains[0].length, 120) && isClose(chains[1].length, 6 * .pi))
    }
}
```

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import CreatorOCCT
import Foundation
import Testing

/// Curve Pattern (patterns spec §3): placements along edges or an outline, facing along the tangent, one branch per chain.
struct CurvePatternNodeTests {
    func rows(_ report: EvaluationReport, _ node: Node) throws -> [[Plane]] {
        let tree = try #require(report.value(node, "placements")?.asTree, "\(String(describing: report.state(node)))")
        return tree.leaves.map { leaf in leaf.items.compactMap { if case .plane(let plane) = $0 { plane } else { nil } } }
    }

    /// A 40 × 20 rectangle outline, wired into a Curve Pattern with `settings`.
    func outline(_ settings: [SocketName: ConstantValue]) async throws -> (rows: [[Plane]], report: EvaluationReport, node: Node) {
        var h = Harness()
        let rectangle = h.add(RectangleNode.self, ["width": .number(40), "height": .number(20)])
        let node = h.add(CurvePatternNode.self, settings)
        h.wire(rectangle, "profile", to: node, "profile")
        let report = try await h.run([node])
        return (report.error(node) == nil ? try rows(report, node) : [], report, node)
    }

    /// The four edges of a 40 × 20 × 6 box that run along X, as an edge set wired into a Curve Pattern.
    func boxEdges(_ settings: [SocketName: ConstantValue]) async throws -> (rows: [[Plane]], report: EvaluationReport, node: Node) {
        var h = Harness()
        let box = h.box(40, 20, 6)
        let along = h.add(EdgesByDirectionNode.self, ["direction": .vector(.unitX)])
        let node = h.add(CurvePatternNode.self, settings)
        h.wire(box, "solid", to: along, "solid")
        h.wire(along, "edges", to: node, "edges")
        let report = try await h.run([node], kernel: OCCTKernel())
        return (report.error(node) == nil ? try rows(report, node) : [], report, node)
    }

    @Test func aClosedOutlineGetsItsPlacementsEvenlyRoundWithoutRepeatingTheStart() async throws {
        let (rows, _, _) = try await outline(["count": .integer(6)])
        let row = try #require(rows.first)
        #expect(rows.count == 1 && row.count == 6)
        // Perimeter 120, a placement every 20 mm from the first corner (−20, −10): along the bottom, up the right, back along the top.
        let expected = [Vector3(-20, -10, 0), Vector3(0, -10, 0), Vector3(20, -10, 0), Vector3(20, 10, 0), Vector3(0, 10, 0),
                        Vector3(-20, 10, 0),
        ]
        #expect(zip(row.map(\.origin), expected).allSatisfy { isClose($0, $1) }, "\(row.map(\.origin))")
    }

    @Test func eachPlacementFacesAlongTheTangent() async throws {
        let (rows, _, _) = try await outline(["count": .integer(6)])
        let normals = try #require(rows.first).map(\.normal)
        let expected = [Vector3.unitX, .unitX, .unitY, -.unitX, -.unitX, -.unitY]
        #expect(zip(normals, expected).allSatisfy { isClose($0, $1) }, "\(normals)")
    }

    @Test func bySpacingStandsThemOneSpacingApartFromTheStart() async throws {
        let (rows, _, _) = try await outline(["mode": .integer(1), "spacing": .number(25)])
        // 120 / 25 = 4 whole spacings round a closed chain: 0, 25, 50, 75.
        #expect(try #require(rows.first).count == 4)
        let second = try #require(rows.first)[1]
        #expect(isClose(second.origin, Vector3(5, -10, 0)))
    }

    @Test func theEdgesOfAFilteredSetFormOneBranchPerChainAndOpenChainsRunEndToEnd() async throws {
        let (rows, report, node) = try await boxEdges(["count": .integer(3)])
        #expect(report.isOK(node), "\(String(describing: report.state(node)))")
        #expect(rows.count == 4, "four parallel edges are four chains")
        for row in rows {
            #expect(row.count == 3)
            #expect(isClose(abs(row[2].origin.x - row[0].origin.x), 40), "from one end to the other")
            #expect(isClose(abs(row[1].origin.x - row[0].origin.x), 20))
            #expect(row.allSatisfy { abs(abs($0.normal.x) - 1) < 1e-9 }, "facing along the edge")
        }
    }

    @Test func aCircleEdgeIsAClosedChainAndItsPlacementsFaceAlongItsTangent() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let circle = h.add(CircleNode.self, ["diameter": .number(20)])
        let extrude = h.add(ExtrudeNode.self, ["distance": .number(10)])
        let edges = h.add(AllEdgesNode.self)
        let node = h.add(CurvePatternNode.self, ["count": .integer(4)])
        h.wire(circle, "profile", to: extrude, "profile")
        h.wire(extrude, "solid", to: edges, "solid")
        h.wire(edges, "edges", to: node, "edges")
        let report = try await h.run([node], kernel: OCCTKernel())
        let rows = try rows(report, node)
        #expect(rows.count == 2, "the rim at each end")
        for row in rows {
            #expect(row.count == 4)
            #expect(row.allSatisfy { abs(Vector3($0.origin.x, $0.origin.y, 0).length - 10) < 1e-6 })
            // Facing along the tangent: at right angles to the radius.
            #expect(row.allSatisfy { abs($0.normal.dot(Vector3($0.origin.x, $0.origin.y, 0))) < 1e-6 })
            #expect(isClose(row[0].origin, -row[2].origin + Vector3(0, 0, 2 * row[0].origin.z)), "opposite quarter-turns")
        }
    }

    @Test func nothingToFollowOrTwoThingsToFollowIsRefused() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let lone = h.add(CurvePatternNode.self)
        let rectangle = h.add(RectangleNode.self)
        let box = h.box(10, 10, 10)
        let edges = h.add(AllEdgesNode.self)
        let both = h.add(CurvePatternNode.self)
        h.wire(box, "solid", to: edges, "solid")
        h.wire(edges, "edges", to: both, "edges")
        h.wire(rectangle, "profile", to: both, "profile")
        let report = try await h.run([lone, both])
        #expect(report.error(lone) == "Wire edges or a profile for the pattern to follow.")
        #expect(report.error(both) == "Wire either edges or a profile to follow, not both.")
    }

    @Test(arguments: [
        (["count": .integer(-1)], "“count” can't be negative."),
        (["mode": .integer(1), "spacing": .number(0)], "“spacing” must be greater than 0 mm."),
        (["mode": .integer(2)], "Choose By count or By spacing for “mode”."),
        (["count": .integer(2_001)], "Patterns are limited to 2,000 instances."),
        (["mode": .integer(1), "spacing": .number(0.01)], "Patterns are limited to 2,000 instances."),
    ] as [([SocketName: ConstantValue], String)])
    func aBadInputIsRefusedInPlainWords(settings: [SocketName: ConstantValue], message: String) async throws {
        let (_, report, node) = try await outline(settings)
        #expect(report.error(node) == message)
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/EdgeCurve+Path.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Measuring and walking a straight or circular edge's curve, for the chains Curve Pattern and Face Pattern follow.
extension EdgeCurve {
    /// A circle that goes all the way round.
    var isFullCircle: Bool {
        if case .circle(_, _, _, _, let sweep) = self { abs(sweep) >= 2 * .pi - 1e-9 } else { false }
    }

    var pathLength: Double {
        switch self {
        case .line(let start, let end): (end - start).length
        case .circle(_, _, let radius, _, let sweep): radius * abs(sweep)
        }
    }

    var startPoint: Vector3 {
        switch self {
        case .line(let start, _): start
        case .circle(_, _, _, let start, _): start
        }
    }

    var endPoint: Vector3 {
        switch self {
        case .line(_, let end): end
        case .circle(let center, let axis, _, let start, let sweep): center + Self.turned(start - center, about: axis, by: sweep)
        }
    }

    /// The same curve run the other way.
    var reversed: EdgeCurve {
        switch self {
        case .line(let start, let end): .line(start: end, end: start)
        case .circle(let center, let axis, let radius, _, let sweep):
            .circle(center: center, axis: axis, radius: radius, start: endPoint, sweep: -sweep)
        }
    }

    /// The point `distance` along from the start and the unit direction of travel there.
    func sample(at distance: Double) -> (point: Vector3, tangent: Vector3)? {
        switch self {
        case .line(let start, let end):
            guard let along = (end - start).normalized else { return nil }
            return (start + along * distance, along)
        case .circle(let center, let axis, let radius, let start, let sweep):
            guard radius > 0, sweep != 0, let k = axis.normalized else { return nil }
            let radial = Self.turned(start - center, about: k, by: sweep * distance / pathLength)
            guard let tangent = k.cross(radial).normalized else { return nil }
            return (center + radial, sweep < 0 ? -tangent : tangent)
        }
    }

    /// `vector` turned counter-clockwise about the unit-or-not `axis` by `angle` radians (Rodrigues' rotation).
    private static func turned(_ vector: Vector3, about axis: Vector3, by angle: Double) -> Vector3 {
        guard let k = axis.normalized else { return vector }
        return vector * cos(angle) + k.cross(vector) * sin(angle) + k * (k.dot(vector) * (1 - cos(angle)))
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/CurveChain.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// A run of straight and circular pieces joined end to end, to place things along (Curve Pattern, patterns spec §3). Each
/// piece runs the way the chain does; a closed chain ends where it began.
struct CurveChain {
    let pieces: [EdgeCurve]
    let isClosed: Bool
    let length: Double

    init(_ pieces: [EdgeCurve], isClosed: Bool) {
        self.pieces = pieces
        self.isClosed = isClosed
        length = pieces.reduce(0) { $0 + $1.pathLength }
    }

    /// The point `distance` along the chain (held to its ends) and the unit direction the chain runs in there. A point where
    /// two pieces meet runs the way the next one does; the end of an open chain, the way its last piece does. `nil` for a
    /// chain with no pieces.
    func sample(at distance: Double) -> (point: Vector3, tangent: Vector3)? {
        var remaining = min(max(distance, 0), length)
        for (index, piece) in pieces.enumerated() {
            if remaining < piece.pathLength || index == pieces.count - 1 {
                return piece.sample(at: min(remaining, piece.pathLength))
            }
            remaining -= piece.pathLength
        }
        return nil
    }

    /// `count` placements spaced evenly: a closed chain gets them all round without repeating its start; an open one puts the
    /// first at its start and the last at its end (a single one stays at the start).
    func distances(count: Int) -> [Double] {
        guard count > 0 else { return [] }
        if isClosed { return (0..<count).map { length * Double($0) / Double(count) } }
        guard count > 1 else { return [0] }
        return (0..<count).map { length * Double($0) / Double(count - 1) }
    }

    /// How many placements `spacing` apart fit: along an open chain from its start, so one on each whole spacing; round a
    /// closed one, without repeating the start.
    func count(spacing: Double) -> Int {
        guard spacing > 0, length.isFinite else { return 0 }
        let fits = (length / spacing + 1e-9).rounded(.down)
        guard fits < Double(Int.max / 2) else { return Int.max / 2 }
        return isClosed ? max(Int(fits), 1) : Int(fits) + 1
    }

    /// The distances of the placements `spacing` apart.
    func distances(spacing: Double) -> [Double] {
        (0..<count(spacing: spacing)).map { Double($0) * spacing }
    }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/EdgeChains.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Joins straight and circular curves into chains (Curve Pattern, patterns spec §3): curves that meet at an end, within
/// `tolerance`, belong to one chain, which runs from one end to the other. Deterministic: the curves are taken in the order
/// given, a chain starts at the first curve with a free end (else the first of all), and at a junction the first curve
/// that continues wins, so a branching set falls into several chains rather than failing.
enum EdgeChains {
    /// How close two ends must be to meet, in millimetres.
    static let tolerance = 1e-4

    static func chains(of curves: [EdgeCurve]) -> [CurveChain] {
        // How many curve ends touch each curve's start and end: 1 (itself) means the end is free.
        let touching: [(start: Int, end: Int)] = curves.map { curve in
            guard !curve.isFullCircle else { return (0, 0) }
            let others = curves.filter { !$0.isFullCircle }
            let count = { (point: Vector3) in
                others.reduce(0) { $0 + (meets($1.startPoint, point) ? 1 : 0) + (meets($1.endPoint, point) ? 1 : 0) }
            }
            return (count(curve.startPoint), count(curve.endPoint))
        }
        var used: Set<Int> = []
        var chains: [CurveChain] = []
        while let first = curves.indices.first(where: { !used.contains($0) }) {
            let free = curves.indices.first { index in
                !used.contains(index) && !curves[index].isFullCircle && (touching[index].start == 1 || touching[index].end == 1)
            }
            let begin = free ?? first
            used.insert(begin)
            guard !curves[begin].isFullCircle else {
                chains.append(CurveChain([curves[begin]], isClosed: true))
                continue
            }
            var pieces = [touching[begin].end == 1 && touching[begin].start != 1 ? curves[begin].reversed : curves[begin]]
            while let tail = pieces.last?.endPoint,
                  let next = curves.indices.first(where: { index in
                      !used.contains(index) && !curves[index].isFullCircle
                          && (meets(curves[index].startPoint, tail) || meets(curves[index].endPoint, tail))
                  }) {
                used.insert(next)
                pieces.append(meets(curves[next].startPoint, tail) ? curves[next] : curves[next].reversed)
            }
            let closes = pieces.count > 1 && pieces.first.map { meets($0.startPoint, pieces[pieces.count - 1].endPoint) } == true
            chains.append(CurveChain(pieces, isClosed: closes))
        }
        return chains
    }

    /// The chains of a profile's loops, outline first and then each hole: a loop is a chain already, closed when its last
    /// segment ends where its first begins.
    static func chains(of profile: Profile2D) -> [CurveChain] {
        profile.loops.filter { !$0.isEmpty }.map { loop in
            let pieces = loop.map { segment -> EdgeCurve in
                switch segment {
                case .line(let a, let b):
                    return .line(start: profile.plane.point(a), end: profile.plane.point(b))
                case .arc(let center, let radius, let start, let end):
                    let startPoint = center + Vector2(cos(start.radians), sin(start.radians)) * radius
                    return .circle(center: profile.plane.point(center), axis: profile.plane.normal, radius: radius,
                                   start: profile.plane.point(startPoint), sweep: end.radians - start.radians)
                }
            }
            let closes = pieces.first.map { meets($0.startPoint, pieces[pieces.count - 1].endPoint) } == true
            return CurveChain(pieces, isClosed: closes)
        }
    }

    private static func meets(_ a: Vector3, _ b: Vector3) -> Bool { (a - b).length <= tolerance }
}
```

`Sources/CreatorNodes/Patterns/Layouts/CurvePatternNode.swift`

```swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// Placements along curves (patterns spec §3): along the edges of an edge set (from the selection nodes) or the outline of a
/// profile, by `count` or by `spacing`, each facing along the curve's direction of travel, with the x axis Plane from Face
/// would give. One branch per connected chain of edges (`EdgeChains`), so a placement is `{chain;index}`; a profile gives its
/// outline first and then each hole.
///
/// By count, the placements are spread evenly: round a closed chain without repeating its start, along an open one from its
/// start to its end. By spacing, they stand one spacing apart from the chain's start (and so fill a closed chain up to the
/// last whole spacing). Straight and circular edges only for now: another curve refuses, naming the edge.
///
/// More than 2,000 placements in all refuses ("Patterns are limited to 2,000 instances.").
public enum CurvePatternNode: NodeDefinition {
    public static let typeID = "creator.curvePattern"
    public static let displayName = "Curve Pattern"
    public static let category = NodeCategory.patterns
    public static let modes = ["By count", "By spacing"]
    public static let inputs = [
        SocketSpec("edges", .edgeSet, access: .list, optional: true),
        SocketSpec("profile", .profile, access: .list, optional: true),
        SocketSpec("mode", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(0)),
        SocketSpec("count", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(8), unit: .count),
        SocketSpec("spacing", .number, defaultValue: .number(10), unit: .millimetres, range: 0.1...200),
    ]
    public static let outputs = [SocketSpec("placements", .plane)]
    public static var singleValuesRefusal: String? { LayoutChecks.singleValues(displayName) }
    public static let inspector = [
        InspectorSection(title: "Along the curve", controls: [.segmented("mode", options: modes), .integer("count"), .slider("spacing")]),
    ]

    public static func evaluate(_ inputs: NodeInputs, kernel: any Kernel, context: EvalContext) async throws -> NodeOutputs {
        let (hasEdges, hasProfile) = (inputs.has("edges"), inputs.has("profile"))
        guard hasEdges || hasProfile else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("Wire edges or a profile for the pattern to follow.") }
        guard !(hasEdges && hasProfile) else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("Wire either edges or a profile to follow, not both.") }
        let bySpacing = try inputs.choice("mode", options: modes) == 1
        let count = try LayoutChecks.count(try inputs.integer("count"), "count")
        let spacing = try inputs.number("spacing")
        guard !bySpacing || (spacing.isFinite && spacing > 0) else { throw NodeError.invalidValue("“spacing” must be greater than 0 mm.") }
        let chains = hasEdges ? try edgeChains(inputs) : try profileChains(inputs)
        // Every chain's count is known before any placement is made, so a pattern that's too big costs nothing.
        var total = 0
        for chain in chains {
            let chainCount = bySpacing ? chain.count(spacing: spacing) : count
            try PatternLimit.check(chainCount)
            total += chainCount
            try PatternLimit.check(total)
        }
        let branches = chains.map { chain -> DataTree in
            let distances = bySpacing ? chain.distances(spacing: spacing) : chain.distances(count: count)
            return .list(distances.compactMap { distance in
                chain.sample(at: distance).map { Scalar.plane(FacePlane.plane(origin: $0.point, normal: $0.tangent)) }
            })
        }
        return NodeOutputs(trees: ["placements": DataTree(depth: 2, branches: branches) ?? .empty(depth: 2)])
    }

    /// The chains of each edge set, set by set: the edges of one solid never join the edges of another.
    private static func edgeChains(_ inputs: NodeInputs) throws -> [CurveChain] {
        var chains: [CurveChain] = []
        for scalar in try inputs.list("edges") {
            guard case .edgeSet(let set) = scalar else { throw NodeError.typeMismatch("edges", expected: .edgeSet) }
            var curves: [EdgeCurve] = []
            for id in set.edges.sorted(by: { $0.rawValue < $1.rawValue }) {
                guard let curve = set.solid.topology.edge(id)?.curve else {
                    throw NodeError.invalidValue("Curve Pattern follows straight and circular edges for now: "
                        + "edge \(id.rawValue.display) is another kind of curve.")
                }
                curves.append(curve)
            }
            chains += EdgeChains.chains(of: curves)
        }
        return chains
    }

    private static func profileChains(_ inputs: NodeInputs) throws -> [CurveChain] {
        try inputs.profiles("profile").flatMap { EdgeChains.chains(of: $0) }
    }
}
```

- Consumes: `Topology.resolution(of: FacePick)`, `FaceInfo`, `EdgeInfo.curve` / `isSeam` / `faces`, Task 8's `EdgeChains` and `EdgeCurve.sample`, `FacePlane`, `NodeSetting.face`, `PatternLimit`, `Topology.vanishedInstances`.
- Produces: `FaceRegion(loops: [[Vector2]])` with `bounds`, `contains(_:)` (even-odd), `distanceToBoundary(_:)`, `allows(_:margin:)`, `insetOutline(margin:)` (mitred); `FaceRegion.outline(of:in:origin:xAxis:yAxis:)` (a flat face's boundary edges joined into loops and cut into chords no more than 0.01 mm from an arc); node type `creator.facePattern` ("Face Pattern": `solid`, `spacingX`, `spacingY`, `stagger`, `margin`, and the stored `face` setting; output `placements`, a tree of depth 3: a branch per row holding a branch per grid cell, each of one placement or none); `FacePatternNode.clippedArea(of:pick:margin:) -> [[Vector3]]` (public, for the preview).

The face is a `FacePick` in `NodeSetting.face` exactly as Plane from Face stores it (the same resolution, the same "merged face" and "unnamed face" warnings, and the same refusal when the pick names a copy that is gone). A curved face refuses: "Face Pattern works on flat faces for now." A face with an edge that is neither straight nor circular refuses to be read. A vent is named by its grid cell, `{row;column;0}`, not by its place among the placements kept (User decision 5): rows count from the lowest row the face's box reaches and columns from the lowest column it reaches (a stagger's half pitch counted in), and a clipped cell is an empty branch, so the margin, a hole's size or its position never renumbers a vent that stays. The candidate cap (200,000 cells) keeps a tiny spacing from hanging. A face narrower than half a pitch with a stagger has rows with no column at all; the loop counts columns with `while`, so no impossible range is formed.
