# --- the spec's errata ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
spec = Path("docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-patterns-trees-design.md")
spec.write_text(spec.read_text().rstrip("\n") + """

## Errata (7a-3: layouts)

Plan `2026-10-12-7a-layouts.md`. Where the build settled what §3, §6 and §8 left open:

- **§3 Shapes.** Linear Pattern is a flat list; Grid, Circular and Curve Pattern are always trees of depth 2 (a branch per row,
  ring or chain), even for one row or ring, so adding one renames no other path. Circular Pattern adds `startAngle`, and its
  per-ring count is a `countStep` added for each ring (no node makes a list of integers to wire in). Curve Pattern follows
  straight and circular edges only (another curve refuses, naming the edge); its chains are curves meeting end to end, in the
  order given, and a profile gives its outline and then its holes. **Face Pattern is a tree of depth 3, a branch per grid cell
  in a branch per row (`{row;column;0}`), not "one branch per row"**: a path counted among the kept placements of a row would
  shift every vent after a dropped one, so a bigger margin, a bigger hole or a smaller face would make a pick on a vent name
  another vent, which §6 forbids. Rows are counted from the lowest row the face's box reaches and columns from the lowest column
  it reaches, a cell that is clipped is an empty branch, and a vent that stays keeps its path whatever the margin or a hole does
  (only a face that grows at its lower left renumbers). It anchors its grid at the face's centroid, keeps a position when it is
  on the face and `margin` clear of every edge (holes included, by true distance), and tries at most 200,000 cells. A list wired
  into a layout's single-valued input is refused in that layout's own words ("Grid Pattern takes single values, not lists: …")
  before it runs, instead of 7a-1's generic tree sentence.
- **§3 Place and the shortcuts over trees.** Placements, tools and sizes are `.tree` sockets, matched by
  `InstanceGrid`: the deepest input sets the shape (a tree's items are the instances); a flat list applies to every branch and
  matches its items; a tree as deep as the shape is matched level by level, a shorter side repeating its last branch; any other
  depth is refused, naming both shapes. The output of Place has the shape of the instances. A tree on `part` or `operation` is
  still refused ("Only placements and sizes can be trees: flatten the tree wired into the part or the operation first."), which
  replaces 7a-2's refusal for everything else. A Boolean fed a tree of copies takes it a branch at a time, as always; Flatten it
  first to cut once.
- **§6 Tags.** An instance's tag item is its path packed by `InstancePath`: `{i}` is the item `i` (so every pick saved before
  trees resolves unchanged), `{a;b}` to four indices each up to 32,767 are one negative number, and a path that can't be
  named is refused. Copies are renamed after the one `Kernel.place` call, which still names them by flat index. Several vanished
  paths are listed in path order.
- **§8 Previews.** A selected layout node shows its placements as an overlay of axis triads (the theme's axis colours; each axis
  is 40% of the smallest distance between neighbours, 5 mm for one placement), Face Pattern also a dashed outline of the face
  moved in by the margin (mitred corners: exact on straight edges, a guide at arcs). A selected layout node is always evaluated, so
  the triads show before anything is wired to it. The triads are an overlay drawn over everything (the sketch editor's mechanism),
  not depth-tested guides like Final preview's selected-rule edges: they never pick, frame or open a menu, but those inside the
  plate show through it, never faded.
- **Pattern on Face.** The face menu of a flat face with a normal also offers "Pattern on Face", which inserts a Face Pattern
  holding the face's pick, wired from the part's producer, as one undo step (refused inside a group, as New Sketch on Face is).
""")

# --- the human checks ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
checks = Path("docs/verification/human-checks.md")
checks.write_text(checks.read_text().rstrip("\n") + """

## Group PL — patterns: the layouts, trees of placements and previews (7a-3)

**Status: NOT RUN.** Plan `2026-10-12-7a-layouts.md`, spec `2026-10-10-patterns-trees-design.md` §3, §6, §8 and its Errata (7a-3). Run
`swift run MetalCreatorApp`. The tests pin the nodes, the naming, the overlay's lines and the file; these check what a real window
shows. Build a plate (Rectangle 120 × 80 on a plane at z = −8, Extrude 8) and an Output for the checks below.

- [ ] **PL-1 The library.** Open the node library: the "Patterns" section opens with Linear Pattern, Grid Pattern, Circular Pattern,
  Curve Pattern and Face Pattern, then Place, Points to Placements and the shortcuts. Typing "ring" in the add-node palette finds
  Circular Pattern. **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-2 Triads.** Add a Grid Pattern (4 × 3, 10 mm) and select it with nothing wired to it: a small x (red), y (green) and z (cyan)
  triad appears at each of the 12 placements, each axis a little under half the distance to the next. Change the counts and the
  spacing in the inspector: the triads follow at once. Select another node, or two nodes: they go. Right-click on a triad, click on
  it, press F and drag the view: it never picks, opens a face menu or changes what F frames. **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-3 How they read.** Do the triads read at the default zoom, on a dark and a light theme (View ▸ Theme ▸ Alucard)? Set a Grid
  Pattern to 40 × 50 (2,000 placements): the view stays smooth. **Record whether the axes are too small or too big** (the 40% rule is
  a guess). Then select a Hole Pattern's Circular Pattern so that its triads sit on the plate's top face and look from below and from
  inside the plate: they are drawn through the solid, never faded (User decision 13). **Record whether that reads or clutters**, and
  so whether depth-aware guides are wanted. **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-4 Rings and per-ring sizes.** Circular Pattern (radius 15, count 4, rings 2, radius step 10, count step 4) into a Hole Pattern
  on the plate (through all), with a Series (6, step −2, 2 values) into a Graft into `diameter`: the inner ring has four Ø6 holes and
  the outer eight Ø4. Pick the rim of one outer hole (Fillet ▸ Pick edges in view…) and fillet it. Set rings to 3: the fillet stays
  on the same hole. Set rings to 1: the Edges by Tag rule warns "Instance {1;3} no longer exists, so the edge picked on it isn't
  selected." (use the path of the hole you picked) and nothing else is filleted. Turn the start angle: the holes and the fillet turn
  together. **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-5 Pattern on Face.** Right-click the plate's top face: the menu offers Look At, Select Edges of Face, New Sketch on Face,
  **Pattern on Face**. Choose it: a Face Pattern appears beside the Extrude, wired from it and selected, its triads fill the face and
  a dashed outline runs round it, 3 mm inside the edge. Switch Stagger on: every second row moves half a pitch. Raise the margin to
  12: the outer columns and rows go. Undo: both the node and the wire go in one step ("Undo Pattern on Face"). Right-click a side
  face of a cylinder: no Pattern on Face. **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-6 Holes in the face.** Drill the plate first (a Hole Pattern, Ø20, through) and use Pattern on Face on its top: no placement
  stands within the margin of the hole, and the dashed outline circles the hole. **Record how the outline looks at the hole** (it is
  a mitred guide; the placements are exact). **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-7 Vents.** Face Pattern (spacing 14 × 12, stagger, margin 6) into a Slot Pattern (8 × 3, through all): staggered vents run
  across the plate, clear of the edge and of the bolt holes. Pick the rim of one vent (Fillet ▸ Pick edges in view…) and fillet it,
  then raise the margin by a few millimetres so some vents drop: the fillet stays on the same vent, or, if that vent has gone, the
  Edges by Tag rule warns naming its path (`{row;column;0}`) and nothing else is filleted. Make a bolt hole bigger: the same.
  **Record which of the two happens to the vents beside the dropped ones** (it must be neither a different vent nor a neighbour).
  **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-8 Curve and Linear.** Edges by Direction (X) on the plate into a Curve Pattern (count 5): five triads along each of four
  edges, facing along the edge. A Rectangle into a Curve Pattern's `profile`: placements round the outline. A Linear Pattern
  (count 6, spacing 8) into a Hole Pattern: a row of holes. **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-9 A Boolean over a tree.** Wire a Grid Pattern into Place and Place into a Boolean's `tools` (subtract from the plate): one
  plate per row, each with its row of copies cut, as for any tree (spec §2). Flatten between Place and the Boolean: one plate with every
  copy cut. **Record whether this is the surprise it may be** (a Hole Pattern over the same grid cuts every hole at once). **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-10 Tree display.** A Grid Pattern's output dot reads "Tree 3 × 4" on rest; the inspector's Data section lists `{0}` to `{2}`
  with 4 items each. A Linear Pattern's reads as a plain list. **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-11 Old files and groups.** Open a bracket saved before this track: it opens with every pick holding and no warning. Group a
  Hole Pattern fed by a Grid Pattern and enter the group: Pattern on Face says it can't be made inside a group yet. **Observed:**
- [ ] **PL-12 A list on a layout.** Wire a Series into a Grid Pattern's `spacingX`: the node refuses with "Grid Pattern takes single
  values, not lists: wire one value into each input, or pick one out of the list first with List Item." and shakes. Put a List Item
  between the Series and the socket: it works. The same for Circular Pattern's `radius`. **Observed:**
""")

**Placeholder scan.** No step says "similar to", "TBD" or describes code instead of showing it: every source and test file is shown whole (new or rewritten files) or as the exact diff against the file on master (existing files that change a little). The code in this plan is the code that was run.

**Type consistency.** `InstancePath.item(for:)` / `indices(ofItem:)` / `text(indices:)` / `text(_:)` / `isOrdered(_:before:)` (Tasks 1, 5) are the names Tasks 2 to 13 use; `InstanceGrid.paths / items / count / tree(of:) / numbers…` (Task 2) are used by Tasks 3 and 4; `PlacementMoves.place / make(_:items:) / requireSingleValues` (Tasks 3, 4); `PatternLimit.refusal` (Task 6) by Tasks 6 to 9; `EdgeChains` and `EdgeCurve.sample` (Task 8) by Task 9; `FacePatternNode.clippedArea` (Task 9) by Task 10; `LayoutPreview.layoutTypes` (Task 10) by `applyPreview`. The node counts (46, `.patterns` 11) and the Patterns library names are updated in the tasks that add nodes (6 to 9), so every task's run is green on its own.

**Review Focus** is covered by the tests the section names. A list wired into a layout's single-valued input (a Series into a Grid Pattern's `spacingX`) is covered too since this revision: it is refused in the layout's own words before the node runs (item 7, User decision 12).

## Risks

1. **Item packing.** A tree path in a tag is a negative `Int`. Nothing indexes an array by a tag item (checked in Sources), but any future code that does must read it through `InstancePath`. The limits (4 indices, 32,767 each) refuse rather than collide (User decision 10).
2. **A list on a layout is refused, not iterated** (User decision 12). A person who wires a Series into a radius to get a ring per value gets a sentence telling them to pick one; the rest of the app broadcasts, so this is a visible difference between nodes. `Evaluator.run` checks `singleValuesRefusal` only when more than one run would happen; a node with none keeps broadcasting.
3. **Boolean over a tree of copies runs per branch** (User decision 11). Correct by spec §2, easy to trip over; human check PL-9 records it.
4. **Face Pattern near arcs, and what renumbers.** Containment and margin use polygons with chords 0.01 mm from an arc, so a placement exactly on a margin boundary round a hole could be kept or dropped by 0.01 mm; the dashed outline is mitred (a guide). A vent is `{row;column;0}`, rows and columns counted from the lowest the face's box reaches (User decision 5): the margin, a hole's size or position and the spacing of a hole never renumber a vent, but a face that grows at its left or bottom past a spacing multiple adds a first row or column and renumbers every vent after it: that is the cost of counting from the box. A spacing change moves the vents (that is what it is for), and `{r;c;0}` then names the cell in the same place in the grid, not the same millimetres. A Graft of sizes for the shortcuts must be depth 3 to be matched level by level with a Face Pattern (depth 2 is refused naming both shapes); a flat list of sizes applies to every cell. A face edge that is a spline refuses.
5. **Overlay cost.** 2,000 placements draw 6,000 overlay lines, rebuilt when the overlay changes (not on orbit, `RendererCacheTests`). Not measured (no benchmark was run). A Face Pattern materialises one empty branch per clipped cell, up to its 200,000-cell cap; only a very large face with a small spacing and a wide margin reaches that, and nothing measured it.
6. **Pattern on Face is top-level only** and a Place inside a group whose tool comes from outside it still doesn't match picks stored in the definition (7a-2's open item, unchanged).
7. **Merging.** This track merges first; the shared files are listed at the top. The counts (46 here, 52 with variation) and the `Patterns` bullet in CLAUDE.md are the likely conflicts; the harness (merging last) conflicts with nothing here, and its total is master + this track + variation + 36. `ContextMenuTests` and anything that lists a flat face's menu entries need "Pattern on Face" after "New Sketch on Face".
8. **`FakeKernel`** cannot revolve, so Hole and Boss tests run on OCCT; Slot, Pattern Feature and Place tests use `FakeKernel` and assert "no error" rather than `isOK`, because its booleans report pieces.
9. **Triads are drawn through the part** (User decision 13), so a dense Hole Pattern's triads inside the plate may clutter the view; PL-3 records it.
